### ClickHouse release 26.10, 2026-10-22

#### Backward Incompatible Change

* The query analysis that preceded the analyzer is removed. It has not been selectable since 26.9, when `enable_analyzer` became obsolete; now a server no longer uses it for a query another server sends either, so a cluster whose initiator is older than 26.10 gets the analyzer on this server regardless of what the initiator asks for. Such a mixed cluster can see `NOT_FOUND_COLUMN_IN_BLOCK` on the old initiator, because the two analyses name the result columns differently; set `enable_analyzer = 1` on every server before upgrading any of them. The `use_analyzer_for_mutations` server setting is removed, and `parallel_replicas_only_with_analyzer` is obsolete. [#120311](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120311) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The predicate push-down that the query analysis preceding the analyzer performed on the AST is removed, and `enable_optimize_predicate_expression` is now obsolete and has no effect. Push-down of a predicate into a subquery is performed by the analyzer as a query plan optimization and is not affected; push-down into a distributed subquery still happens on the AST level and is controlled by `allow_push_predicate_ast_for_distributed_subqueries`. The visible difference is that `EXPLAIN SYNTAX` of a query that is not a `SELECT`, for example `EXPLAIN SYNTAX INSERT INTO ... SELECT ...`, no longer prints a predicate copied into a subquery, a non-aggregate `HAVING` conjunct moved to `WHERE`, or the right-hand table of a `JOIN` wrapped into a subquery. [#121465](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121465) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `system.metric_log` now uses the `bucketed` schema by default: all profile events and current metrics are stored in a single `metrics` column of type `Map(Enum16(...), Int64)` with the bucketed `Map` serialization and a constant number of 128 buckets, so the table has a few columns instead of thousands, zero values are not stored, and reading one metric reads only one of the 128 buckets. Every metric is also exposed as an `ALIAS` column, so queries such as `SELECT ProfileEvent_Query FROM system.metric_log` continue to work unchanged. On upgrade the existing table is renamed to `metric_log_0`, as with any other change of the structure of a system log table, and the built-in dashboards read `merge('system', '^metric_log')`, so they keep showing the data collected before the upgrade. Add `<schema_type>wide</schema_type>` to the `<metric_log>` section of the server configuration to keep the previous schema. [#118318](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118318) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#124016](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124016) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* `clickhouse-server` can now apply a `seccomp` system call filter at startup. The `seccomp` setting controls refused system calls: `log` only records them, while `trap`, `kill`, and `errno` enforce the policy; `disabled` installs no filter. The `config.xml` shipped with the server packages and the Docker image now sets `seccomp` to `trap`, so a server that runs in a sandbox which refuses the `seccomp` system call must switch it to `log` or `disabled` before the upgrade. Extended-attribute probes used by some hostname-resolution libraries are rejected with `ENOSYS` instead of the configured seccomp action, so server startup still succeeds under the default `trap` mode. You might need to disable seccomp when using executable user-defined functions. [#119545](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119545) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#122577](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122577) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#124856](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124856) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* Settings constraints and `allow_feature_tier` are now enforced for users, roles and settings profiles defined by SQL. A statement is refused if it changes a restricted setting of some user indirectly (such as `GRANT`, `DROP ROLE`, `DROP SETTINGS PROFILE` or `CREATE USER OR REPLACE`), sets a value outside its author's constraints, or removes a setting its author is constrained on. A user defined by SQL whose stored settings, roles or assigned settings profiles violate the constraints or `allow_feature_tier` can no longer log in. The `compatibility` setting no longer applies values that settings constraints or `allow_feature_tier` refuse; such settings keep their current value. Login checks use the user's resolved constraints, preserving bounds inherited from the default profile. Closes [#124966](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/124966). [#119472](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119472) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)). [#125582](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125582) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Overriding a key that is stored in a named collection now requires `SHOW NAMED COLLECTIONS SECRETS` on that collection. This also applies to overrides through an alias (including flat `kafka_*` settings over nested `kafka.*` keys), through a `SETTINGS` clause of a table engine or table function, and through keys in a dictionary source (checked when the dictionary is created). Resolving a relative `url` stored in a named collection against `s3_base` or `url_base` also counts as an override of `url`. Adding a key that the collection does not store remains allowed, and keys marked `NOT OVERRIDABLE` cannot be overridden. The `allow_named_collection_override_by_default` setting is obsolete. Existing tables whose `SETTINGS` or alias arguments override a `NOT OVERRIDABLE` key cannot be loaded after the upgrade, and the server does not start: before upgrading, recreate such tables without the override, or make the key overridable. [#123133](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123133) ([Nikita Mikhaylov](https://github.com/nikitamikhaylov)).
* `max_bytes_before_external_join` and `max_bytes_ratio_before_external_join` are now the spill threshold for every hash-based `join_algorithm`, including `grace_hash`, which previously ignored them and spilled on `max_rows_in_join` / `max_bytes_in_join` instead. Those two settings are a hard cap again for every algorithm: they no longer cause spilling, and a join that spills no longer exceeds them. All join algorithms remain available; `join_algorithm = 'grace_hash'` now needs a non-zero spill threshold, and is skipped in favour of the next algorithm in the list when it has none. Set `legacy_join_size_limits_trigger_spilling = 1` (or `compatibility` to 26.8 or older) to restore the previous behavior. [#117356](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117356) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)). [#119335](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119335) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `arrayCount` now returns `UInt64` instead of `UInt32`, so it stays exact for arrays with more than 4294967295 matching elements. Set `array_count_legacy_uint32_result = 1` (or `compatibility = '26.9'`) to restore the previous result type. During a rolling upgrade, enable `array_count_legacy_uint32_result` on upgraded servers for the users under which shard-side queries execute, so distributed queries initiated by not-yet-upgraded servers keep the previous behavior on upgraded shards; remove it after the upgrade is complete. In addition, `length(arrayFilter(func, arr))` is now rewritten to `arrayCount(func, arr)`, which is up to 1.29x faster on long arrays, controlled by `optimize_rewrite_array_filter_length_to_array_count`. [#113023](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113023) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `accurateCast` from `String` or `FixedString` to a `Nullable` target now throws the error its non-`Nullable` target already raised (`CANNOT_PARSE_TEXT`, `TOO_LARGE_STRING_SIZE`, ...) instead of returning `NULL`, and no longer throws for a row the source marked `NULL`. This affects every use of `accurateCast`: `DeltaLake` writes stop committing an unrepresentable value as `NULL`, `dictGetOrDefault` rejects a default its `Nullable` attribute cannot hold and now converts only the defaults it uses, `IN` under `transform_null_in = 1` no longer matches an unparsable key against a `NULL` in the set, and `cast_ipv4_ipv6_default_on_conversion_error` yields the type's default for an unparsable address. `CAST`, `accurateCastOrNull`, `accurateCastOrDefault`, and ordinary `INSERT` are unchanged, as is what a non-text source does with a value it cannot represent; use `accurateCastOrNull` for the previous behavior. Closes [#120524](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/120524). [#120674](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120674) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `SELECT ... FINAL` from `SummingMergeTree` no longer removes the rows whose summed columns are all zero, unlike background merges. Before, this removal looked only at the columns the query read, so results depended on them: for example, `SELECT count() FROM table FINAL` could return fewer rows than `SELECT * FROM table FINAL`. Background merges and `OPTIMIZE TABLE ... FINAL` still remove such rows, so filter out the all-zero rows explicitly if a query relied on their removal. [#115032](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115032) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A read of a `Distributed` or `Remote` table with `skip_unavailable_shards = 1` now fails with `ALL_CONNECTION_TRIES_FAILED` when every shard was skipped before returning any data, instead of returning an empty result, matching what already happens when the only shard is local. Previously an aggregate without `GROUP BY` silently returned zero rows instead of one. The error fails the whole query, so a query that combines such a read with other data, for example a `UNION ALL` with local rows, no longer returns those rows. When a shard is itself a `Distributed` table whose shards were all skipped, the outer query now fails even if another shard answered; `skip_unavailable_shards_mode = 'unavailable_or_exception_before_processing'` keeps the partial result in that case. No setting restores the previous empty result. Closes [#115646](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/115646). [#115775](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115775) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `MATERIALIZED` column computed from a column with a `TTL` is now recomputed when the `TTL` resets that column, both during a merge and in `ALTER TABLE ... MATERIALIZE TTL`. Previously it kept the pre-expiry value forever. If the dependent expression is not defined on the expired column's default (for example `intDiv(100, x)` when `x` expires to `0`), the `TTL` merge now throws instead of writing the stale value; make the expression total on the default (`intDivOrZero`, `if(x = 0, ...)`) if such a table must keep merging. Closes [#117310](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/117310). [#117815](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117815) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* With `input_format_null_as_default = 0`, a JSON `null` for a non-`Nullable` numeric column now raises an exception instead of being silently stored as `0` (or `nan`), consistent with other types and formats. Keep `input_format_null_as_default` at its default `1` to insert the column default instead. [#122137](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122137) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` and `MODIFY COLUMN` now apply the `data_type_default_nullable` setting the same way `CREATE TABLE` does: with the setting enabled, a column type written without an explicit `NULL` or `NOT NULL` modifier becomes `Nullable`, so the rows that existed before `ADD COLUMN` read `NULL`, `ADD COLUMN x Int32 DEFAULT NULL` is accepted, and `MODIFY COLUMN x Int64` keeps a `Nullable` column `Nullable` instead of failing. As in `CREATE TABLE`, an `Array`, `Map`, `LowCardinality`, or another type that cannot be inside `Nullable` then requires `NOT NULL`, and so does a `MODIFY COLUMN` of a sorting key column. To keep the previous result of such an `ALTER`, write `NOT NULL` explicitly or run it with `data_type_default_nullable = 0`. Closes [#124504](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/124504). [#124597](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124597) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `arrayJoin` is now lowered to an `ARRAY JOIN` step by default (setting `query_plan_lower_array_join_function`). The expansion streams through the pipeline instead of being materialized inside the projection, filters on the joined elements are applied before the rows are multiplied, and queries with several `arrayJoin` calls no longer build the whole cross product in memory. As a consequence, a non-deterministic function such as `rand` or `generateUUIDv4` next to `arrayJoin` in the same `SELECT` is now evaluated once per output row, as with the `ARRAY JOIN` clause, instead of once per source row. Set `arrayjoin_nondeterministic_functions_before_expansion = 1` (or `compatibility` to 26.9 or older) to keep the previous behavior. [#117882](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117882) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)). [#120390](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120390) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)). [#122240](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122240) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* A function that depends on blocks or row order, such as `runningDifference`, `neighbor` or `rowNumberInBlock`, next to the `arrayJoin` function now sees the blocks of the expansion, like with the `ARRAY JOIN` clause. Set `legacy_array_join_function_nondeterministic_evaluation = 1` to get the previous behavior. [#123143](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123143) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* A row policy on a `Distributed` table (or another table that processes the query itself) read through a `Merge` table now makes queries to the `Merge` table fail with `ILLEGAL_PREWHERE` for the users the policy applies to, like queries to that table itself. Before, the `Merge` table applied such a policy after the table had already sorted, limited and computed `extremes`, so queries could return wrong rows and show values of hidden rows in `extremes`. Define the row policy on the underlying tables of the remote servers and on the local child tables instead. This also fixes `NOT_FOUND_COLUMN_IN_BLOCK` on every query over a `Merge` table whose child has a row policy when the `Merge` table also reads a `Distributed` table. [#125765](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125765) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `clickhouse-local --ignore-error` now reports a statement that failed at execution instead of skipping it silently: previously it printed no message and no exception code, so a failing multi-statement script looked exactly like a passing one, while `clickhouse-client --ignore-error` reported every error all along. `clickhouse-client` also ignored the option entirely when it was combined with `--interactive` and `--query`/`--queries-file` on a terminal, and now applies it to those queries too. The exit code of `clickhouse-client --ignore-error` changes accordingly: it used to be the code of whichever error the last statement of the batch happened to hit, which made the result depend on the position of the failing statement rather than on the batch as a whole, and it is now a success code, matching what `clickhouse-local --ignore-error` has always returned. Scripts that relied on the old exit code to detect failures should drop `--ignore-error` so that the run stops and fails at the first error, or check the reported errors on `stderr`. [#119572](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119572) ([Yakov Olkhovskiy](https://github.com/yakov-olkhovskiy)).
* `ATTACH TABLE ... FROM '<path>'` now requires `READ ON FILE` and `WRITE ON FILE` on the source directory in `user_files`, in addition to `CREATE TABLE`, because the query reads the directory and then moves it out of `user_files` on success. Previously a user could attach and read staged data without those `FILE` grants. Grant the corresponding `FILE` privileges to users that rely on this form. [#120582](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120582) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* A `MySQL` dictionary source created with a `CREATE DICTIONARY` query can no longer enable `enable_local_infile`, at the source or at one of its `REPLICA` entries; loading such a dictionary fails with `BAD_ARGUMENTS`, which with the default `dictionaries_lazy_load` is on first use rather than at `CREATE`. A `REPLICA` that does not set the key inherits the source's value, as the connection pool does. To keep the option, define the dictionary in a server configuration file; `enable_local_infile = 0`, and a `SOURCE(MYSQL(NAME ...))` reference to a named collection where the key was never read, are unaffected. [#120972](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120972) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The definitions of disks in `storage_configuration` and in `disk` are validated: an element that nothing reads is reported as `UNKNOWN_ELEMENT_IN_CONFIG` instead of being silently ignored, which finds typos and options that do not exist for this disk type, such as `path` in an `S3` disk. If a server does not start after the upgrade because of an element it does not know, remove that element from the configuration, or set `<skip_check_for_incorrect_settings>1</skip_check_for_incorrect_settings>`. [#118440](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118440) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `system.*_log` tables can now be configured uniformly via `system_tables.*`. In `system.opentelemetry_span_log`, `finish_date` is renamed to `event_date` and kept as an alias. Aliases are now supported in configured `PARTITION BY`, `ORDER BY`, `TTL`, `PRIMARY KEY`, and `SAMPLE BY` expressions, so custom definitions can continue to use `finish_date`. Some tables now fall back to the default TTL when none is configured, so set `ttl` explicitly if that matters. [#121563](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121563) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)). [#125564](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125564) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* The setting `validate_mutation_query` is deprecated and ignored: mutation queries (`ALTER TABLE ... UPDATE`, `ALTER TABLE ... DELETE`, `DELETE FROM`) are now always validated before being accepted. It was enabled by default since 24.11, and disabling it did not avoid the failure but only deferred it to the background, where the mutation got stuck and required manual intervention. Setting it to `0` no longer lets an invalid mutation be accepted: it is rejected when submitted. [#121419](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121419) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Settings `query_plan_read_in_order`, `query_plan_optimize_prewhere`, and `query_plan_aggregation_in_order` are obsolete and no longer have any effect. Use `optimize_read_in_order`, `optimize_aggregation_in_order`, and `optimize_move_to_prewhere` instead. [#121390](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121390) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* The setting `use_compact_format_in_distributed_parts_names` is obsolete. The non-compact directory format for the async `INSERT` queue of a `Distributed` table has been removed. The setting was `1` by default since version 21.1. Before upgrading, set it to `1` and let the queue drain. [#120186](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120186) ([Alexander Tokmakov](https://github.com/tavplubix)).
* The `MergeTree` settings `use_minimalistic_part_header_in_zookeeper` and `use_minimalistic_checksums_in_zookeeper` are obsolete and have no effect: new data parts of `ReplicatedMergeTree` tables are always stored in ZooKeeper as a single node with the compact header, the default since 20.1. An explicit `0` in table metadata or in the `merge_tree` configuration is ignored, and such tables and configurations still load. Part nodes written in the old format by earlier versions are still read. Closes [#122744](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122744). [#122790](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122790) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Settings `s3_max_connections` and the `S3` disk's `max_connections` key are obsolete. They have had no effect since 21.4, when the per-endpoint connection pool of the `S3` client they bounded was removed, but were still documented as limiting the number of connections per server. Both are still accepted and do nothing. To bound the connections to object storage, use the server settings `disk_connections_soft_limit`, `disk_connections_store_limit`, `disk_connections_warn_limit`, and `disk_connections_hard_limit` and their `storage_connections_*` and `http_connections_*` counterparts; to bound the request rate, use `s3_max_get_rps` and `s3_max_put_rps`. [#119550](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119550) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Removed the zero-argument `year` function. Queries that used `year()` must switch to `toYear(today())` instead. [#120268](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120268) ([Robert Schulze](https://github.com/rschu1ze)).
* Removed the `system.codecs.is_experimental` column. Queries that filtered or selected it should use `tier = 'Experimental'` instead. [#118523](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118523) ([Raufs Dunamalijevs](https://github.com/rienath)).
* Removed the experimental function `detectTonality`. Queries that use it now fail with `UNKNOWN_FUNCTION`. [#123327](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123327) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Update the `zxc` library to v0.14.0, which changes the compressed block format of the experimental `ZXC` codec. Data written with `CODEC(ZXC)` by earlier versions cannot be read by the new version and fails with `CANNOT_DECOMPRESS`. If you used the experimental `ZXC` codec, re-encode the affected columns to a different codec before upgrading, or re-insert the data after the upgrade. [#119097](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119097) ([Actuele AI](https://github.com/actueleai)).
* An aggregate function state that serializes to zero bytes cannot be read back, so the two parameterizations that produced one are now rejected: a `-Resample` combinator over an empty range (`begin >= end`, such as `countResample(10, 5, 1)`) throws `ARGUMENT_OUT_OF_BOUND`, and `covarSampMatrix`, `covarPopMatrix`, and `corrMatrix` with no arguments throw `NUMBER_OF_ARGUMENTS_DOESNT_MATCH`. Both returned an empty array before. No serialized format changes, but a table with such a column no longer loads. Before upgrading, drop the column where the engine supports `DROP COLUMN`, and the whole table where it does not (`Log`, `TinyLog`, `StripeLog`, `Set`, `Join`, `File`). [#119728](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119728) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A data type naming the state of a function that only works as a window function, such as `AggregateFunction(rank)`, is now rejected with `BAD_ARGUMENTS`: such a state has no serialization, so the column could never hold a value. This applies wherever a type is named, including inside `Array`, `Map`, `Tuple`, and another state's argument list. A table that already stores such a column will no longer attach after the upgrade; drop or rewrite the column before upgrading. [#122148](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122148) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `rankState` and every other `-State` of a function that only works as a window function, such as `denseRankStateIf` or `initializeAggregation('lagInFrameState', x)`, are now rejected with `BAD_ARGUMENTS` when the query is analyzed, also where the state is never computed (`toTypeName`, `EXPLAIN`, a column the outer query drops), and so is such a type in a binary-encoded `Native` header. Computing such a state always failed, but a view or `CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT` could still store its type. A table whose engine expressions build such a state, for example in a sorting key, partition key, skip index, or `TTL`, no longer attaches. [#122463](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122463) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `CREATE TABLE` and full-definition `ATTACH TABLE` now refuse a `CONSTRAINT` name declared more than once, and `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT` / `MODIFY CONSTRAINT` now refuse an expression containing `arrayJoin` even when the command would install nothing. A table already stored with a repeated constraint name still attaches and keeps enforcing each of its declarations; give each constraint a unique name in new definitions. [#121235](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121235) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The `Remote`, `RemoteSecure`, and `Cluster` database engines now reject an empty remote database name with `BAD_ARGUMENTS` instead of creating a database that lists and reads no tables. A server that holds such a database, created by an earlier version, does not start after the upgrade, so drop these databases before upgrading. Closes [#122616](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122616). [#122675](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122675) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Connections to MySQL (the `mysql` table function, the `MySQL` table and database engines, and `MySQL` dictionaries) that set `ssl_ca` or `ssl_ca_pem` now verify the server certificate chain and host name, after updating `mariadb-connector-c` to v3.4.10. Previously the CA was not enforced, so a connection to a server whose certificate is signed by another CA, or whose certificate does not list the host name or IP address used to connect, succeeded and now fails with `self-signed certificate in certificate chain` or `Hostname verification failed`. Before upgrading, make sure the configured CA signs the MySQL server certificate and that the certificate lists the host name or IP address ClickHouse connects to. [#119510](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119510) ([Actuele AI](https://github.com/actueleai)).

#### New Feature

* Sets built for `IN` with a subquery or a table now spill to disk by default once query memory usage exceeds half the available memory under the applicable server or user memory limit, calculated at the start of execution (`max_bytes_ratio_before_external_set = 0.5`). The `max_bytes_before_external_set` setting can set an absolute threshold (`max_memory_usage` is not taken into account by the ratio, so use it to spill before that limit is reached), and setting both thresholds to `0` keeps sets in memory. This feature is now generally available. [#123860](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123860) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)). [#124907](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124907) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Support parts of the Trino SQL dialect in regular ClickHouse SQL: `TRY_CAST(x AS T)`, the `ARRAY[...]` array constructor, the type names `ARRAY(T)` and `MAP(K, V)`, and aliases for Trino functions such as `starts_with`, `slice`, `regexp_like`, `day_of_week`, `url_extract_host`, `bitwise_and`, `arbitrary`, and `bool_and`. [#124763](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124763) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Implemented a generic block nested loop join algorithm. `JOIN` now supports an arbitrary `ON` condition with no equality between the two tables, executing it as a block nested loop join, as slow as a `CROSS JOIN` with a filter, instead of failing with `INVALID_JOIN_ON_EXPRESSION`. The new setting `allow_block_nested_loop_join` turns it on/off. [#123642](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123642) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#123792](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123792) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Added the function-composition operator `f | g` and the argument placeholders `_`, `_1`, `_2`, ...: for example, `arrayMap(plus(_, 1) | multiply(_, 2), [1, 2, 3])` returns `[4, 6, 8]`. A composition can be used wherever a function is expected, including as an argument of a higher-order function. The operands can be lambdas, function names, nested compositions, or expressions with placeholders, which also work on their own, for example `arrayMap(_1 * 2 + 1, [1, 2, 3])`. The composition is resolved at query analysis time into an ordinary lambda. Note that `|` here is not bitwise OR; use `bitOr` for that. [#116029](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116029) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The query cache can now store its entries on disk, backed by a preconfigured filesystem cache: this gives the entries more space, makes them survive server restarts, and, with a distributed filesystem cache, provides a way to share them between servers. Select the filesystem cache with the new setting `query_cache_on_disk_cache_name`; see also the new settings `query_cache_on_disk_codec`, `enable_writes_to_query_cache_on_disk`, and `enable_reads_from_query_cache_on_disk`. Closes [#115361](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/115361). [#115382](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115382) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added a notebook mode to the Web UI (`/play`). After a query is run, a `[+]` button under the result adds further cells, each either a query with its own result or a block of Markdown text, turning the tab into a notebook in the style of Jupyter or Observable. Cells can be run, reordered, inserted and deleted individually; the query editor collapses to a single line when it is not focused, results are bounded and scroll inside themselves, and the whole notebook is restored on reload. Notebook mode is per tab, so a tab that has only one cell looks and behaves exactly as before. [#115378](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115378) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added support for reading and writing data in the `SQLite` format. [#104510](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/104510) ([Yue Ni](https://github.com/niyue)).
* Added the `Cluster` database engine, which provides real-time access to the tables of a configured cluster database and forwards `SELECT` and `INSERT` queries to it. [#114417](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114417) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the `Overlay` database engine: `CREATE DATABASE db ENGINE = Overlay(db1, db2, ...)` exposes the union of the tables of existing databases, each table working as an `Alias` table to the table of the first source database that has it. [#121878](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121878) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `CREATE DATABASE` and `ATTACH DATABASE` now accept a `SETTINGS` clause without an explicit `ENGINE` clause, defaulting to `Atomic`, so a database can define its metadata disk inline the same way a single table does. A single `SETTINGS` clause can now also mix database-engine settings with ordinary query settings. [#118423](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118423) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `MergeTree` tables in `Ordinary` databases can now be converted to `ReplicatedMergeTree` with `ATTACH TABLE ... AS REPLICATED` or the `convert_to_replicated` flag. [#118757](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118757) ([Kirill](https://github.com/kirillgarbar)).
* Added query-aware scheduling in workloads: the new workload setting `scheduler` selects how queries of a workload are scheduled — `fifo` (default), `fair` (weighted fair queueing with per-query weights that can be lowered as a query runs), `las` (least-attained-service), or `priority` (by the query setting `workload_priority`). The leaf-node `type` in `system.scheduler` is now `request_queue` instead of `fifo`. [#116800](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116800) ([Sergei Trifonov](https://github.com/serxa)).
* Queries can now spill to disk under memory pressure based on limits set per workload. The `max_memory_before_spill` and `max_memory_to_spill_ratio` options of `WORKLOAD` define a soft memory limit that triggers spilling for `ORDER BY`, `GROUP BY`, `IN`, `DISTINCT`, and `JOIN` with `grace_hash`. [#119443](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119443) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* Quotas can now limit the number of rows written by `INSERT` queries with the new `written_rows` resource, for example `CREATE QUOTA q KEYED BY ip_address FOR INTERVAL 1 minute MAX written_rows = 100000, query_inserts = 250 TO user`. Until now only the written bytes could be limited (`written_bytes`), which is awkward when the natural unit of the workload is rows. The current and maximum values are exposed as `written_rows` and `max_written_rows` in `system.quota_usage`, `system.quotas_usage`, and `system.quota_limits`, and the resource can also be set in `users.xml` as `<written_rows>`. [#118272](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118272) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the settings `max_temporary_tables`, `max_temporary_table_memory_usage`, `max_temporary_table_size_bytes_compressed`, and `max_temporary_table_size_bytes_uncompressed` to limit the number of temporary tables in a session, the memory usage of `Memory` temporary tables, and the size of `MergeTree` temporary tables. [#122165](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122165) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the SQL-standard `SIMILAR TO` pattern-matching predicate. [#101514](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/101514) ([Zhao Zheguang](https://github.com/zheguang)).
* Allow `MATERIALIZED` common table expressions inside recursive `WITH`. A materialized helper CTE is evaluated once and every recursive step reads the snapshot, instead of the subquery being re-evaluated on each iteration. The recursive CTE itself still cannot be materialized. [#123452](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123452) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Query parameters can now be used for primary `USER` and `ROLE` names in `CREATE`, `ALTER`, and `DROP`, and for role and grantee names in `GRANT` and `REVOKE`, for example `GRANT {role:Identifier} TO {user:Identifier}`. [#109696](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/109696) ([Pervakov Grigorii](https://github.com/GrigoryPervakov)).
* `SET DEFAULT ROLE` now accepts an `ON CLUSTER` clause, in either position: `SET DEFAULT ROLE role1 TO user ON CLUSTER my_cluster` and `SET DEFAULT ROLE ON CLUSTER my_cluster role1 TO user`. `CURRENT_USER` is resolved on the initiator before the query is distributed. `SET ROLE` only affects the current session and still takes no `ON CLUSTER` clause. [#118429](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118429) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added `CREATE OR REPLACE NAMED COLLECTION ...` to replace an existing named collection entirely. [#121569](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121569) ([Pervakov Grigorii](https://github.com/GrigoryPervakov)).
* The `postgresql` table function and the `PostgreSQL` table engine can now connect to another ClickHouse server over the PostgreSQL protocol. The `query(...)` variant still needs an explicit column list. Added the PostgreSQL-compatibility functions `format_type` and `current_setting`. Also fixed cancellation over the PostgreSQL protocol: a cancel request never cancelled anything. [#112940](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/112940) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Disks with the `plain_rewritable` metadata (`s3_plain_rewritable`, and `azure`/`local` object storages with `metadata_type = plain_rewritable`) support hard links if configured with the new `enable_hard_links` setting, which enables `ALTER` operations and mutations on `MergeTree` tables stored on them. Mutations and partition operations share the unchanged blobs between parts instead of copying them. The setting is off by default, because a disk that has hard links cannot be read by older servers. [#110939](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110939) ([yanglongwei](https://github.com/ylw510)). [#119304](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119304) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added `EXCEPT DATA FROM TABLE` / `EXCEPT DATA FROM TABLES` clauses to `BACKUP` queries, so a table's definition can be backed up without its rows, for any table engine that supports backups. Formatting now quotes keyword table names correctly in `EXCEPT DATA FROM TABLES`, so `BACKUP ... ON CLUSTER` round-trips through the formatter. Closes [#111827](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/111827). [#111895](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/111895) ([Aditya Kumar](https://github.com/adityaksolves)). [#122468](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122468) ([David Meng](https://github.com/davidmenggx)).
* Added five columns to `system.query_log` describing what a query really executed: `used_number_of_joins`, `used_join_algorithms`, `used_join_kinds`, `used_join_strictness`, and `spilled_to_disk`. This makes it possible to find problematic `JOIN` patterns and queries that fall back to external memory across a fleet without reproducing each query. A spilling `SELECT DISTINCT` now records `distinct` in `spilled_to_disk` instead of `[]`, and a spilling block nested loop join records `join`. [#113833](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113833) ([Matheus Nerone](https://github.com/Manerone)). [#121830](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121830) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)). [#124681](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124681) ([David Meng](https://github.com/davidmenggx)).
* Added table function `traceView` that renders an OpenTelemetry trace from `system.opentelemetry_span_log` as a call tree with a per-span timeline bar, for debugging where the time of a traced, for example distributed, query was spent. It has a `host` column with the `hostname` of the server that recorded each span, so the spans of the initiator, remote shards, replicas, and stateless workers can be distinguished and filtered with `WHERE host = ...`. [#116325](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116325) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)). [#122169](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122169) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)). [#123528](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123528) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)).
* `clickhouse-client --login` can now authenticate against arbitrary OAuth/OIDC identity providers via endpoint discovery, with explicit endpoint and scope overrides and improved RFC 8628 device-code handling. On Linux desktops it now also passes the client's environment to `xdg-open` when opening the identity-provider login page, so the system browser is detected correctly. Closes [#120127](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/120127). [#110718](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110718) ([yanglongwei](https://github.com/ylw510)). [#121337](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121337) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Added interactive client commands `/dialect`, `/lang`, and `/language` to `clickhouse-client` and `clickhouse-local`, so you can switch the active SQL dialect before parsing, including when the current dialect cannot express a `SET` query, for example to leave `kusto` or `promql`. The current non-default dialect is now shown in the prompt, and `SET dialect = ...` keeps the prompt in sync. [#114568](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114568) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `chdig` [v26.10.1](https://github.com/azat/chdig/releases/tag/v26.10.1): ZooKeeper browser, per-host summary, and better filters. [#124100](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124100) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* Support reading the `Parquet` `VARIANT` type (unshredded, as written for example by Spark 4.0) into `Dynamic`, with object values read as `JSON`. A variant column can also be read directly as `JSON` if all its values are objects. Shredded variants are not supported yet. [#117106](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117106) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Support the `variant` type of `Iceberg`. Based on the `Parquet` variant reader in [#117106](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117106). [#117198](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117198) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)). [#125458](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125458) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Support the `Parquet` `FILE` logical type (a reference to bytes stored inline or in an external file): it is read as a named `Tuple`, and can be written from a `Tuple` with the corresponding element names with the new setting `output_format_parquet_write_file_logical_type`. [#125132](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125132) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Lazy type hints for the `JSON` type are now generally available under the `enable_json_lazy_type_hints` setting. The setting remains disabled by default. [#122711](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122711) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* Add a new input format `Freeform`, which does schema inference for arbitrary tabular text files. Closes [#34006](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/34006). [#43449](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/43449) ([Tri Dao](https://github.com/dmtri35)).
* The `generateRandom` table function and the `GenerateRandom` table engine now support `JSON`, `Dynamic`, `Variant`, `BFloat16`, `Time`, and `Time64`. Generated `JSON` documents have a realistic schema derived from the seed. The new settings `null_ratio`, `max_json_depth`, and `max_json_keys_per_object` control the share of nulls and absent keys, the nesting depth, and the number of keys per object. Generation respects memory limits. `CREATE TABLE ... AS generateRandom(...)` also keeps the `SETTINGS` argument in the stored definition. [#107966](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/107966) ([apst41](https://github.com/apst41)). [#124772](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124772) ([Vadim Lopatin](https://github.com/buggins)). [#123247](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123247) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Add the `obfuscate` table function for generating anonymized query results. [#115004](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115004) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Text indexes with the `keyValuePairs` tokenizer now answer `map['key'] IN ('value1', 'value2')` directly from the index, as they already did for `map['key'] = 'value'`. Added the function `mapContainsKeyValue(map, key, value)`, which returns whether a map contains an entry with the given key and value; in contrast to `map[key] = value`, all entries of a repeated key are considered, and such an index evaluates the function directly. The `mergeTreeTextIndex` table function returns the decoded parts of each token of such an index in the columns `token_key` and `token_value`. [#120244](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120244) ([Jimmy Aguilar Mena](https://github.com/Ergus)). [#120010](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120010) ([Jimmy Aguilar Mena](https://github.com/Ergus)). [#120183](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120183) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* The `mergeTreeTextIndex` table function now returns posting lists and token positions for a text index in the new virtual columns `row_id` and `positions`. If one of them is selected, the function returns a row per posting. The `positions` column is available for indexes created with `support_phrase_search = 1`. [#125536](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125536) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Added `mapRemove`, which removes all entries with a specified key from a map, and `mapEntries`, which returns a map's entries as an array of key-value tuples. [#123322](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123322) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)). [#123344](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123344) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* The `-Map` combinator can now be used with non-`Map` columns (the key is passed as the last argument, e.g. `sumMap(value, key)`) and supports aggregate functions with multiple arguments, e.g. `argMaxMap(arg, val, key)`. [#123449](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123449) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The aggregate functions `avg`, `sum`, `sumWithOverflow`, and `sumKahan` now support the `Interval` data types. The result is an interval of the same unit. [#121458](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121458) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#121600](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121600) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The linear regression aggregate functions can now also be called under ClickHouse-style names: `regrSlope`, `regrIntercept`, `regrR2`, `regrCount`, `regrAvgX`, `regrAvgY`, `regrSXX`, `regrSYY`, and `regrSXY`. [#119796](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119796) ([mosya415](https://github.com/mosya415)).
* Added the `arrayMinIndex` and `arrayMaxIndex` array functions. [#120097](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120097) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)). [#123686](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123686) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Added the `arrayRemoveAt` array function. [#120445](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120445) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Added higher-order array functions to pack bits: `arrayPackBitsToUInt64`, `arrayPackBitsToString`, `arrayPackBitsToFixedString`, `arrayPackBitGroupsToUInt64`, `arrayPackBitGroupsToString`, and `arrayPackBitGroupsToFixedString`. [#123478](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123478) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added SQL functions `normalizedQueryHashUnordered` and `normalizedQueryHashUnorderedOrNull`. Unlike `normalizedQueryHash`, they ignore the order of expression lists such as the `SELECT` list, `GROUP BY` keys, and function arguments, so logically equivalent queries can collapse to one pattern in `query_log`; the `OrNull` variant returns `NULL` instead of throwing when the input is not valid ClickHouse SQL in the current session. [#116345](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116345) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Add functions `encodeQuotedPrintable` and `decodeQuotedPrintable` for Quoted-Printable encoding (RFC 2045), commonly used in email. [#125454](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125454) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* Added the `sql_compatible_division_for_integers` setting, disabled by default, to make `/` and `divide` return integer quotients truncated toward zero when both operands are integers. It supports integer types up to 256 bits and checks division by zero and result overflow; division involving `Decimal` or floating-point operands is unchanged. [#124490](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124490) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Added `SYSTEM RESET FILELOG [db.]table [FILE 'file_name' [OFFSET n | TO END]]`: a `FileLog` table reads all its files again from the beginning, or one file from the beginning, from a given offset, or only from data appended after the command, without recreating the table. Requires the new `SYSTEM RESET FILELOG` privilege. [#123545](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123545) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The `FileLog` table engine now accepts globs (`*`, `?`, `{a,b}`, `{N..M}`) in the file name of its path, for example `FileLog('/var/lib/clickhouse/user_files/my_app/*.log', 'JSONEachRow')`, and reads only the matching files of that directory, so the compressed files `logrotate` leaves next to the log are not read. A file that is already read keeps being read after it is renamed to a non-matching name, such as `app.log` rotated to `app.log.1`. [#123590](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123590) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Added the `flat_namespaces` setting to the `DataLakeCatalog` database engine. Set it for `Iceberg` REST catalogs that have only single-level namespaces and reject the `parent` query parameter of the list-namespaces endpoint, such as Apache Polaris federated to AWS Glue: only top-level namespaces are then listed and sub-namespaces are not requested. [#117329](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117329) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Added the ClickHouse Keeper `ListWithOptions` operation for filtered direct or recursive listing with optional stats, data, limits, and shuffled results. [#117521](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117521) ([Antonio Andelic](https://github.com/antonio2368)).
* Added the `MergeTree` setting `min_unreserved_disk_space_for_merge`: background merge selection subtracts the specified amount from the unreserved disk space, so large merges stop being selected before they can take the last free space and starve inserts. Unlike `keep_free_space_bytes`, the protected space remains usable by ClickHouse itself. [#115723](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115723) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* You can now disable reading through the `executable` table function and from `Executable`/`ExecutablePool` tables with the new `allow_executable_tables` setting, for a deployment that must not run arbitrary server-side scripts. It defaults to `true`, so existing behavior is unchanged. [#120713](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120713) ([Hanzi Jiang](https://github.com/HanziJiang)).
* Added server setting `ai_function_allow_sql_named_collections`. When disabled, AI functions accept only named collections defined in the server configuration file. [#124999](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124999) ([George Larionov](https://github.com/george-larionov)).
* Added `<openSSL><server><cipherSuites>` and `<openSSL><client><cipherSuites>` settings to restrict which TLS 1.3 cipher suites a TLS port negotiates. Without them, `cipherList` controls only TLS 1.2 and below. An absent or empty value keeps the OpenSSL defaults. Closes [#119929](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/119929). [#120480](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120480) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* You can now set a custom syslog program name for ClickHouse logs using `logger.syslog.programname`, making it easier to distinguish multiple ClickHouse instances in syslog. The default syslog identifier is now command-specific: remote syslog previously sent `-` as the RFC 5424 `APP-NAME` and now sends the command name (for example `clickhouse-server`), and the multi-purpose binary started as `clickhouse server` or `clickhouse keeper` now uses `clickhouse-server` / `clickhouse-keeper` instead of `clickhouse` for local syslog. [#98789](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/98789) ([François Martin](https://github.com/martinfrancois)).
* Added the `Copy` event and the `source_bucket` and `source_remote_path` columns to `system.blob_storage_log` for server-side object copies (S3 `CopyObject` and `UploadPartCopy`, Azure Blob Storage copies). Azure backups, restores, and `AzureQueue` moves now write to `system.blob_storage_log` too. Closes [#123163](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123163). [#123178](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123178) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Added an `is_lazy` column to `system.dictionaries`. It shows whether each dictionary loads on first use, taking into account both its `dictionary_lazy_load` setting and the server setting `dictionaries_lazy_load`. This makes it possible to tell a lazy dictionary that has not been used yet from an eager one that has not been loaded yet; both show `NOT_LOADED`. [#124260](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124260) ([Miсhael Stetsyuk](https://github.com/mstetsyuk)).
* Added the `replica_group` column to `system.clusters`. For clusters that belong to a `Replicated` database, it shows the replica group of each node as published from the server configuration `replica_group_name`, or its alias `role`. [#120537](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120537) ([RinChanNOW](https://github.com/RinChanNOWWW)).
* Added `SYSTEM DISABLE ALL FAILPOINTS`, which disables every fail point at once and resumes any thread blocked on a pauseable one. [#118877](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118877) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).

#### Experimental Feature

* Added an experimental columns cache that keeps deserialized columns of wide `MergeTree` parts in memory, one entry per stripe of granules of a column. Compared with the uncompressed cache, this saves time on deserialization and copying. It is enabled by the experimental setting `use_columns_cache`, with `enable_reads_from_columns_cache` and `enable_writes_to_columns_cache` as beta sub-settings, and it applies to tables in databases that assign UUIDs (`Atomic` and `Replicated`). The cache gives its memory back to queries when the server is short of it. [#96844](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/96844) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added experimental `LATERAL JOIN` support, allowing correlated subqueries in the `FROM` clause. This enables the standard SQL pattern for "top N per group" and per-row aggregation queries. Controlled by the `allow_experimental_lateral_join` setting. [#97833](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/97833) ([Dimitri König](https://github.com/dimitri-koenig)).
* Added an experimental `logsql` dialect: LogsQL, the log query language of VictoriaLogs, can now be used to query ClickHouse tables with logs. Enable it with `SET enable_logsql_dialect = 1, logsql_table = '<table>', dialect = 'logsql'` (plus `logsql_database`, `logsql_time_column`, and `logsql_message_column` as needed). LogsQL queries are translated at parse time into ordinary `SELECT` queries, so text filters expect `String`-backed log columns, while the `math` pipe applies raw arithmetic and needs numeric operand columns (`String` field values are not coerced to numbers). [#113207](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113207) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#123891](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123891) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the beta `PCO` compression codec for fixed-width numeric columns, based on the [pcodec](https://github.com/pcodec/pcodec) library. Enable it with `enable_pco_codec = 1`. [#110613](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110613) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added a new implementation of the `unity` catalog type in the `DataLakeCatalog` database engine. It serves both `DeltaLake` and `Iceberg` tables from a single Unity Catalog, detecting the format of each table and routing `Iceberg` tables through the Unity Catalog `Iceberg` REST endpoint. Enable it with the database setting `use_unity_catalog_v2`, or set the session setting of the same name before `CREATE DATABASE`. Switch an existing database with `ALTER DATABASE ... MODIFY SETTING use_unity_catalog_v2 = 1`. [#115879](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115879) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)).
* Added an experimental embedded `Iceberg` REST catalog server with ClickHouse HTTP authentication. It supports `GET /v1/config`, listing, creating, and checking the existence of namespaces, stored either in memory or in `Keeper`, and listing, creating, loading, checking the existence of, and dropping tables, with their metadata files stored in `S3`. The catalog can also serve TLS via `secure_port`, using the certificate and key in `openSSL.server`, and correctly handles `/` in table and namespace names. Closes [#125762](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/125762). [#118924](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118924) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)). [#123000](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123000) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)). [#123331](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123331) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)). [#124978](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124978) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)). [#125791](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125791) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Added experimental XGBoost dictionaries: the `XGBOOST` layout trains a gradient-boosted model from the source table at load time, and `predictXGBoost` runs inference. Enable it with `enable_xgboost`. Available only in builds that include the XGBoost contrib library. [#109999](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/109999) ([Matheus Nerone](https://github.com/Manerone)).
* Added `ipcryptEncrypt`, `ipcryptDecrypt`, `ipcryptPrefixEncrypt` and `ipcryptPrefixDecrypt` functions for format-preserving encryption of IP addresses following the [IPCrypt specification](https://ipcrypt-std.github.io/). The deterministic mode produces consistent output for deduplication, while the prefix-preserving mode maintains subnet relationships. Enable with `SET enable_ipcrypt = 1`. Contributed by Frank Denis. [#123680](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123680) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added read support for `Iceberg` v3 deletion vectors stored in `Puffin` files. [#110781](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110781) ([linjiayu1025-collab](https://github.com/linjiayu1025-collab)).
* Added experimental support for `ALTER TABLE ... DROP PARTITION` on standalone `Iceberg` tables and `Iceberg` REST catalogs. On standalone tables, the operation currently requires `Iceberg` format-version 2 with a single non-evolved partition spec. Old date-transform spellings in the partition expression are now evaluated with the `Iceberg` transform semantics too. [#111219](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/111219) ([Den Kalantaevskii](https://github.com/Diskein)). [#123273](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123273) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)). [#122269](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122269) ([Den Kalantaevskii](https://github.com/Diskein)).
* `parallel_replicas_plan_based` can now distribute queries with an `IN (subquery)` predicate instead of falling back to single-node execution. Closes [#111876](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/111876). [#114086](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114086) ([Igor Nikonov](https://github.com/devcrafter)).
* With `parallel_replicas_plan_based` enabled, distributed `INSERT ... SELECT` again uses distributed execution when `parallel_distributed_insert_select = 2`, instead of running the `SELECT` on the initiator alone. Closes [#118276](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/118276). [#121097](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121097) ([Igor Nikonov](https://github.com/devcrafter)).
* Distributed query plans built with `make_distributed_plan` now compress exchanged data with `network_compression_method`; for `ZSTD`, they also use `network_zstd_compression_level` instead of the server's default codec settings. [#120333](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120333) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* Queries executed with the experimental `make_distributed_plan` setting now propagate the OpenTelemetry trace context to stateless workers via the `traceparent` and `tracestate` HTTP headers of the task dispatch request. Each dispatched task produces a `StatelessWorkerClient::sendTask` span on the initiator plus `InterserverIOHTTPHandler` and `DistributedPlanTask::execute` spans on the worker, with the task id, initial query id, plan hash, and exchange streams as attributes, so the whole distributed plan is visible as one trace in `system.opentelemetry_span_log`. [#119010](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119010) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)).
* Join runtime filters can now travel across distributed query plan stages with the experimental setting `distributed_plan_join_runtime_filters` (default off). Build-side tasks send partial filters to probe-side scans before their shuffle exchange, reducing the number of rows crossing the network. A filter that is lost, late, or never built cannot change results. [#114822](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114822) ([Harikrishnan Prabakaran](https://github.com/harikrishnan94)).
* Distributed index analysis is now beta. [#123577](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123577) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* Added the experimental setting `enable_join_seal_gated_reading` (default off). When enabled together with `enable_join_runtime_filters` and the probe-side join key is a prefix of the table's primary key, the probe side of a hash `JOIN` starts reading only after the build side completes, and the completed runtime filter prunes whole mark ranges by the primary key before read tasks are created, instead of only filtering rows after they were read. [#113512](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113512) ([Nikolai Kochetov](https://github.com/KochetovNicolai)).
* Replaced the experimental settings `query_plan_optimize_join_order_use_conflict_detector_a` and `query_plan_optimize_join_order_use_conflict_detector_c` with `query_plan_optimize_join_order_conflict_detector`, which takes `a`, `c`, or `''` (the default). [#120630](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120630) ([Fisnik Kastrati](https://github.com/fkastrati)).
* Adaptive codec selection (`enable_adaptive_codec_selection`) also tries each specialized codec followed by the table's default codec, and makes merges of `Float32` and `Float64` columns faster. [#121630](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121630) ([Raufs Dunamalijevs](https://github.com/rienath)). [#122248](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122248) ([Raufs Dunamalijevs](https://github.com/rienath)).
* Added the setting `json_extract_named_tuples_as_objects` (default off): when enabled, the `JSONExtract` functions fill named tuples from JSON objects only. By default a JSON array fills a named tuple positionally, so which element lands in which field depends on the order the fields were declared in. [#122637](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122637) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Experimental `UNIQUE KEY` SST probes now batch lookups with `MultiGet`, so absent keys can be filtered by the SST bloom filter. More unsupported `UNIQUE KEY` operations are now rejected early. [#119997](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119997) ([johnjing](https://github.com/JingYanchao)). [#123002](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123002) ([Murphy](https://github.com/murphy-4o)).
* The experimental reader executor (`use_reader_executor = 1`) now plans filesystem cache reads ahead over a window set by `reader_executor_plan_look_ahead` instead of re-checking the cache on every read window. It also no longer fails spuriously with `CANNOT_READ_ALL_DATA` when a concurrent download advances the filesystem cache during a cold read. [#115816](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115816) ([Sema Checherinda](https://github.com/CheSema)). [#122214](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122214) ([Sema Checherinda](https://github.com/CheSema)).
* Streaming queries now prefer a `commit_order` projection when one is available for the current read round. [#120420](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120420) ([Mikhail Artemenko](https://github.com/Michicosun)).
* PromQL again supports the range functions `present_over_time`, `absent_over_time`, `quantile_over_time`, and `predict_linear`, and now also supports `stddev_over_time`, `stdvar_over_time`, `first_over_time`, `ts_of_first_over_time`, `ts_of_last_over_time`, and `mad_over_time`. [#112355](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/112355) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)). [#120352](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120352) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)). [#121578](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121578) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)). [#121800](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121800) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* Added PromQL functions `min_of` and `max_of` to return the smaller or larger of two scalar values. [#121250](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121250) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* PromQL now implements `timestamp` for both direct selectors and general expressions, matching Prometheus 3.5.0 semantics. [#112356](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/112356) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* Support standalone PromQL `step` and `range` scalar functions. The PromQL parser now also accepts bare calls to `start`, `end`, `sort_by_label`, `sort_by_label_desc`, `double_exponential_smoothing`, and `limit_ratio`, so these not yet implemented functions are rejected after parsing instead of failing with a parse error. [#121118](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121118) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)). [#123589](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123589) ([James Cunningham](https://github.com/JTCunning)).
* `prometheusQuery` now always returns `Float64` values and `DateTime64` timestamps with at least millisecond precision. The time zone of the returned timestamps comes from its `DateTime` or `DateTime64` arguments when they agree, and otherwise from the table. [#120285](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120285) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* PromQL now treats Prometheus stale markers as absent in instant selectors and omits them from range selectors. [#121098](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121098) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* PromQL now rejects queries that use the same label in both `on(...)` and `group_left(...)` or `group_right(...)`. [#114376](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114376) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* PromQL now rejects vector selectors whose matchers can all match the empty string. [#121099](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121099) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* PromQL's `stddev` and `stdvar` now return `NaN` instead of `0`. [#122743](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122743) ([James Cunningham](https://github.com/JTCunning)).
* PromQL now returns `NaN` from date functions when the sample is not finite. [#124300](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124300) ([James Cunningham](https://github.com/JTCunning)).
* PromQL no longer fails on empty joins. [#124362](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124362) ([James Cunningham](https://github.com/JTCunning)).
* Aligned PromQL string and comment parsing with Prometheus: quoted strings cannot contain a line feed, and a line comment can end at the end of the query without a space after `#`. Closes [#117812](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/117812). [#124584](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124584) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* PromQL range functions over scalars now handle windows with no samples without failing on `NULL`, including when used in nested range functions. [#124586](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124586) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* Added deduplication caches for `TimeSeries` metric families and tags, and fixed stale cache entries when the pending rows are full. [#121915](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121915) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)). [#122771](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122771) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* The `tags` inner table of the `TimeSeries` table engine is split into a `tags` table without `min_time` and `max_time` and a separate table of time ranges. [#121924](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121924) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* The inner tables of the `TimeSeries` table engine now use the outer column name `metric_family` instead of `metric_family_name`. [#119226](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119226) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* Fixed `ALIAS_REQUIRED` for a join with a `VIEW` written without an alias under the experimental setting `analyzer_inline_views = 1`: the inlined subquery now keeps the view's name as a table expression name, so it needs no alias and stays usable as a column qualifier, with or without the database name. [#118401](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118401) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Reading subcolumns of the experimental `Quantized` codec (`vec.quantized`, `vec.product_quantization_codebook`) through a `Merge` table no longer breaks when another child table of the `Merge` lacks the base column. [#123895](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123895) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).

#### Performance Improvement

* Large hash tables used for `GROUP BY`, `uniqExact`, and `JOIN` now use up to two times less memory, and aggregation with many keys is faster on many threads. [#125001](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125001) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The query condition cache now serves conditions that involve the current time, e.g. `WHERE time >= now() - INTERVAL 10 DAY`, when reading from `MergeTree` tables, by deriving a deterministic condition from them with the current-time constant rounded onto a time grid. Closes [#115504](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/115504). [#115522](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115522) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Enabled the setting `apply_string_filters_during_scan` by default. Substring search conditions on `String` columns (`LIKE`, `position`, `startsWith`, `endsWith`) from `PREWHERE` are pushed down into the column scan, and non-matching values are read as empty strings without copying their data. This makes such queries faster and lowers their memory usage when reading from `MergeTree` tables and from `Parquet` pages whose string values use the `PLAIN`, `DELTA_LENGTH_BYTE_ARRAY`, `DELTA_BYTE_ARRAY`, or dictionary encodings, including reads using the uncompressed cache on object storage. [#115848](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115848) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#124503](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124503) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#125079](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125079) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)). [#124507](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124507) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Queries `GROUP BY key LIMIT n` and `GROUP BY key ORDER BY key LIMIT n` over `MergeTree` tables now read much less data: the aggregation publishes the boundary of its top-K heap to the reading step, which drops rows beyond it before reading the other columns and can skip whole granules through the primary key or a `minmax` skip index. Controlled by the new setting `enable_group_by_top_k_dynamic_filtering`. The same primary-key granule skipping now also applies to `ORDER BY column LIMIT n` when the column is in the primary key. [#121145](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121145) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Queries with `ORDER BY <column> LIMIT n` over `Parquet` files now use TopN dynamic filtering with `use_top_k_dynamic_filtering`: rows that cannot enter the top-K are filtered inside the reader before the remaining columns are read, and whole row groups are skipped when their min/max statistics prove they cannot contribute. This works through the `file` and `url` table functions, the `File` and `URL` engines, object storage (`s3`, `Azure`, `HDFS`), and data lake tables such as `Iceberg`. Floating-point sort columns are excluded for now. [#115798](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115798) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#122109](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122109) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#124861](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124861) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `query_plan_join_shard_by_pk_ranges` is enabled by default. A `full_sorting_merge` or `parallel_full_sorting_merge` `JOIN` of two `MergeTree` tables whose join keys contain a prefix of the primary key of both tables now runs as independent streaming merge joins over primary-key ranges on all threads instead of a single-threaded merge join, several times faster and with several times less memory in our tests. Hash joins are affected only when both join runtime filters and spilling to disk are disabled. Set `query_plan_join_shard_by_pk_ranges = 0` to restore the previous behavior. [#117856](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117856) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#119925](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119925) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Enabled the text-index postings cache by default with `use_text_index_postings_cache`. On a 1B-row table, a four-token phrase query that previously recomputed posting lists and phrase-search results within one query went from 19 s to 0.12 s. The shared cache is bounded by `text_index_postings_cache_size` (2 GiB by default). [#120677](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120677) ([Nikita Fomichev](https://github.com/fm4v)). [#123228](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123228) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* With `optimize_aggregation_in_order = 1`, or with the default `optimize_distinct_in_order = 1`, a `GROUP BY` or `DISTINCT` whose keys extend past the table's sorting key no longer re-packs the whole block's grouping key once per run of equal sorting-key values. That cost was quadratic in `max_block_size` once the sorting prefix is near-unique, so `GROUP BY a, b` on a table sorted by `a` is up to 130x faster. Closes [#45386](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/45386). [#120259](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120259) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Blocks sent back by remote replicas are now marshalled in parallel (`enable_parallel_blocks_marshalling`) when `parallel_replicas_plan_based` is enabled. [#121642](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121642) ([Igor Nikonov](https://github.com/devcrafter)).
* `GROUP BY` with a `Nullable` key now uses the adaptive aggregator, except when that key is the only one and is a number of up to 8 bytes, a `String`, or a `FixedString`, or when any key is `LowCardinality`. Schema inference makes such keys common in CSV, JSON, and `Parquet` files; high-cardinality aggregations over them become up to 2x faster with up to 2.2x less memory. [#124868](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124868) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Queries over `Nullable` columns now use the same optimizations as over non-`Nullable` ones: `length`, `empty`, `notEmpty`, and comparisons with an empty string on `Nullable(String)` read the `size` subcolumn, `sum(x + c)` is rewritten to `sum(x) + c * count(x)`, and `uniqExact` over `Nullable` is merged in parallel. Some queries become several times faster. [#125011](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125011) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The trivial `GROUP BY ... LIMIT n` optimization (setting `optimize_trivial_group_by_limit_query`) now also applies to queries with aggregate functions in the projection when the `GROUP BY` top-K optimization does not apply, for example above `query_plan_max_limit_for_top_k_optimization`: once any aggregation thread exceeds `n + offset` distinct keys, all threads are restricted to a single shared set of kept keys, so the hash tables stay small while the aggregate values of the returned keys remain exact, with up to 7-9x speedups. The optimization no longer fires for queries with `DISTINCT`, `QUALIFY`, `LIMIT AFTER`, `LIMIT UNTIL`, window functions, `arrayJoin`, or `exact_rows_before_limit`, where it could return wrong or inexact results. [#114690](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114690) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* When `max_threads` is not set explicitly, a query is planned with the fair share of CPU slots of the concurrency control instead of all CPU cores. This increases the throughput and lowers the memory usage under many concurrent queries, while a query on an idle server still uses all cores. It can be disabled with the setting `use_concurrency_control_for_max_threads`. [#125085](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125085) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A single-level `GROUP BY` result smaller than `max_block_size` is now split into several blocks when it was aggregated by several threads, so the steps after it, such as the probe of a following join, run in parallel instead of on one thread. Previously such queries could be several times slower, most visibly under `compatibility` older than 26.8, which disables `enable_parallel_single_level_merge` and `enable_adaptive_aggregator`. The result of a `GROUP BY` on a single 8- or 16-bit integer key is split the same way. [#121711](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121711) ([Pratham Nayak](https://github.com/protomn)).
* Restored the batched hash-and-prefetch insert path for `uniq`, `uniqCombined`, and `uniqCombined64` in ordinary `GROUP BY` queries, where they had silently fallen back to a per-row loop and were up to 46% slower.
