Closes [#120531](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/120531). [#120578](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120578) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster `GROUP BY` over keys that have to be serialized, such as several columns that include a `String`. [#122857](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122857) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* `GROUP BY` over multiple `LowCardinality` keys now serializes the keys from the per-block dictionaries instead of copying them into full columns first, reducing CPU time and memory usage of aggregation queries over `LowCardinality` dimensions. [#121795](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121795) ([artemkulyk](https://github.com/artemkulyk)).
* Speed up `GROUP BY` over several `LowCardinality` keys when the same key tuples repeat, by caching aggregate states per tuple of dictionary positions in the serialized aggregation method. [#121998](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121998) ([artemkulyk](https://github.com/artemkulyk)).
* `GROUP BY` over a single `String` column with only `count` is faster when the grouping state does not fit in the CPU cache, especially with long strings. [#123655](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123655) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `GROUP BY` queries with many groups that end in `ORDER BY count() ... LIMIT n` and use no other aggregate function are slightly faster when they group by a `String` column or by several columns. [#124277](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124277) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Share the `GROUP BY` top-K skip boundary between aggregation threads. [#116150](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116150) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Allow using fused `GROUP BY` top-K with a serialized query plan. [#116163](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116163) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Added a query-plan optimization, controlled by the new setting `query_plan_aggregation_having_prefilter`, for `HAVING count() <comparison> <constant>` over a `GROUP BY`. While a two-level bucket of the aggregation result is converted to chunks, a group whose `count()` cannot satisfy the bound is now skipped before its key columns are materialized, instead of being materialized and then discarded by the filter above. This speeds up "groups above a threshold" queries over a high-cardinality `GROUP BY` with wide keys, where the discarded keys are most of the conversion. A skipped group is neither filtered nor finalized, so a `HAVING` conjunct written before the bound, and a sibling aggregate's finalization, stop being evaluated on the groups the bound rejects: a query that raised an exception from one of those can now return rows instead. Setting `query_plan_aggregation_having_prefilter = 0`, or `compatibility` to a version below `26.10`, keeps the previous behavior. [#120510](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120510) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `optimize_aggregators_of_group_by_keys` now works for `LowCardinality` `GROUP BY` keys, so a `HAVING` predicate such as `HAVING min(s) = 'x'` is pushed down to storage and can use skip indexes, as for a plain key. It also eliminates `anyHeavy`, `quantileExact`, `quantileExactLow`, `quantileExactHigh`, `groupBitAnd`, and `groupBitOr` of a `GROUP BY` key. [#110059](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110059) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `-If` aggregate functions with one `Nullable` argument no longer allocate a buffer the size of the whole block for every group with `optimize_aggregation_in_order` or for every window frame. With small groups or frames this makes them 1.7 to 3.1 times faster on x86\_64. [#122505](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122505) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up `-ForEach` aggregate functions such as `sumForEach` and `avgForEach`: rows are processed in batches instead of calling `add` for each row, and an array of two or more elements is passed to the nested function in one call instead of one call per element. Grouped multi-argument `-If` aggregate functions with a `Nullable` argument or condition are also processed in batches. [#121137](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121137) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)). [#124207](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124207) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up serialization of `AggregateFunction` state columns with a fixed-size state, such as `sum`, `count`, `avg`, `min`, `max`, and `any`, and their `-If`, `-Array`, `-Merge`, and `Nullable` variants. This affects writes to `AggregatingMergeTree`, the `Native` format, distributed queries, and spilling aggregate states to disk. [#113962](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113962) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Speed up reading and merging of `uniq` aggregate function states, such as `uniqMerge` over an `AggregatingMergeTree`, by 10-35% depending on state sizes and merge shape. [#111493](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/111493) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Faster merging of `uniqCombined` and `uniqCombined64` states with many distinct values into empty states, e.g. in `-Merge` combinators and `AggregatingMergeTree`: up to 6x faster. [#124389](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124389) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Reduce memory usage and improve performance of external `DISTINCT` by avoiding spills for duplicate-heavy input, writing smaller spill blocks, budgeting tail spilling before the final merge, preserving constant payloads, combining chunks before sorting, and retaining fewer rows while restoring order with `LIMIT`. [#121772](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121772) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Faster sorted-stream processing on a multi-column key whose leading column has long runs, in `LIMIT BY`, negative `LIMIT BY`, `DISTINCT` in order, `full_sorting_merge` and `partial_merge` `JOIN`, and `ReplacingMergeTree` `FINAL` without vertical `FINAL`: the run of a key prefix is now found once instead of again for every group in it. The query from [#123700](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123700) is about 3.5x faster. [#123808](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123808) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Short queries with many keyed aggregations or scalar subqueries over large constant-folded literals are faster because hash-table-stats cache keys no longer hash whole variable-size constant values. [#122738](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122738) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Reduce memory usage of some window functions by precomputing peer-group boundaries instead of keeping earlier blocks in memory. [#123737](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123737) ([Mikhail Artemenko](https://github.com/Michicosun)).
* A filter on the `PARTITION BY` columns of a window function is no longer pushed below the window (this reverts [#110114](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110114)). The push-down made queries up to 8 times slower when the window reads a `JOIN` and the filter removes every row, for example `IN` over an empty subquery, because the join built its right side before the filter could stop the query. Closes [#123875](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123875). [#125463](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125463) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `hash` and `parallel_hash` joins now copy right-side output columns directly from the stored blocks instead of calling the stored column object once per row. This speeds up every column type, including `String`, `Array`, `Tuple`, `Nullable`, `Variant`, `Map`, `LowCardinality`, `JSON`, `Dynamic`, `AggregateFunction`, and `QBit`. [#117460](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117460) ([Harikrishnan Prabakaran](https://github.com/harikrishnan94)).
* Speed up hash join output construction in the row-major layout: output reconstruction is up to 2x faster for joins with many rows per key and about 1.5x for wide or `Nullable` payloads. The row-major layout is now also skipped for payloads too narrow to benefit from it. [#121981](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121981) ([Hechem Selmi](https://github.com/m-selmi)).
* Hash joins with many matches per key are faster when they output a `LowCardinality` column whose blocks have different dictionaries (several parts, `FINAL`, or a `LowCardinality` value computed per block): short ranges from another dictionary are now inserted key by key instead of through temporary columns. Such joins run 4-9x faster, at the 26.6 level or better. [#123900](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123900) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `RIGHT` and `FULL` joins using `hash`, `parallel_hash`, or `grace_hash` skip the search for unmatched right rows when every right key has matched. With 50 million right keys, that search was about 10% of query time for `hash` (356 ms of 3.6 s) and about 15% for `parallel_hash` (106 ms of 0.7 s). [#124775](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124775) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `full_sorting_merge` join now advertises that its result is ordered by the join key, so a sort above it, the pre-sort of the next merge join in a chain or an `ORDER BY`, merges the already sorted streams instead of sorting from scratch. Joining several tables on the same key with `full_sorting_merge` no longer performs one full sort per join. Also fixed the interplay with `query_plan_join_shard_by_pk_ranges`, which could otherwise fail with a logical error or return wrong results for such a chain. [#117786](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117786) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A join step that has a condition of its own now also joins on equalities implied through other tables, so for `a.x = b.x AND b.x = c.x`, joining `a` with `c` also uses `a.x = c.x`. With `query_plan_optimize_join_order_algorithm = 'dpsize'`, this makes TPC-H Q02 join its correlated subquery to `partsupp` on the part key instead of on the supply cost alone. Closes [#122396](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122396). [#122445](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122445) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `WHERE` condition that no row satisfies is pushed to every `JOIN` input again, including the non-preserved side of an `OUTER JOIN` and a side with no column used above the join, so that input is no longer read and hash-built for nothing. For example, a `UNION ALL` branch whose constant column fails the outer `WHERE` is skipped as in 26.6. Closes [#123702](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123702). Closes [#123878](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123878). [#123829](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123829) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Constant comparison, logical, and null (`IS NULL` / `IS NOT NULL`) filters over materialized constants are now folded and pushed down, reducing unnecessary reads in `WHERE`, `PREWHERE`, and `UNION` branches. Always-true `WHERE` filters are replaced with expressions so join reordering still sees all joined tables as one graph. [#105987](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/105987) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)). [#124056](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124056) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* `OUTER JOIN` queries can now be faster: when a `WHERE` clause or another `JOIN` later in the query would discard the unmatched rows anyway, ClickHouse converts the join to an `INNER JOIN`. The new setting `query_plan_convert_outer_join_to_inner_join_transitively` controls this, and only takes effect when `query_plan_convert_outer_join_to_inner_join` is enabled. [#120253](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120253) ([Hechem Selmi](https://github.com/m-selmi)).
* Restored filter push-down over `ANY INNER JOIN`, so primary-key pruning works again and such queries no longer read much more data than necessary. Because `ANY` keeps an arbitrary matching row, `SELECT ... ANY INNER JOIN ... WHERE ...` can now return a different matching row than before. [#120765](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120765) ([Kirill Shokhin](https://github.com/kirillshokhin)).
* `optimize_and_compare_chain` again derives transitive conditions through a `DateTime` or lower-scale `DateTime64` constant compared with a `DateTime64` expression, as it did before 26.8. For example, `t1.ts < toDateTime('2026-09-16 00:00:00') AND t1.ts >= t2.ts` adds `t2.ts < toDateTime('2026-09-16 00:00:00')`, so the other side of a join can be pruned by its partition and primary key instead of being read in full. Closes [#123706](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123706). [#123796](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123796) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Improve join order selection when column statistics are missing: an equi join without NDV statistics is now treated more like a foreign-key-to-primary-key join instead of a cross product, and the greedy join-order algorithm prefers equi-connected pairs over pairs linked only by non-equi predicates. [#120176](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120176) ([Vladimir Cherkasov](https://github.com/vdimir)). [#121075](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121075) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Improves `JOIN` cardinality estimation by propagating join-key distinct-value estimates according to join semantics. Outer joins retain the preserved-side estimate, anti joins keep their input estimates, and semi joins narrow only the output side, preventing underestimation that could lead to suboptimal query plans. [#122305](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122305) ([Christoph Viebig](https://github.com/cv4g)).
* `IEJoin` now treats opposite-direction conditions on unrelated columns as independent conditions instead of as a band, which avoids choosing a join-key pair that passes thousands of times more rows to the residual condition. [#120213](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120213) ([Vladimir Cherkasov](https://github.com/vdimir)).
* Function `firstNonDefault`, which computes the `USING` columns of `FULL JOIN`, now copies whole ranges of rows instead of making two virtual calls per row. Queries that use a `USING` column of a `FULL JOIN` after the join, for example in `WHERE`, are faster. [#123302](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123302) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Enabled `enable_join_runtime_filters_index_analysis` by default, so join runtime filters can prune granules during the probe phase. [#121859](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121859) ([Robert Schulze](https://github.com/rschu1ze)).
* Join runtime filters now run after the query's own `PREWHERE` conditions also for tables without column statistics, as they already did with statistics. Before, a runtime filter on a well-compressed join key could run first and read that key for every row, so enabling `enable_join_runtime_filters` could make a query read several times more data than without it. [#122452](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122452) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The granule pruning of `enable_join_runtime_filters_index_analysis` now also applies when the left side of a `JOIN` is read from a normal projection. The setting description states that the pruning does not happen with parallel replicas, a distributed query plan, or `FINAL`. [#119041](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119041) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The query condition cache now records the static `WHERE` conditions of a join whose `PREWHERE` also holds a join runtime filter, so a repeated join skips the granules those conditions exclude instead of reading them again. This helps whenever the runtime filter cannot skip those granules through an index itself, for example when no primary key or skip index covers the join key, or when `enable_join_runtime_filters_index_analysis` is disabled. [#122380](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122380) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Repeated insertion of empty and single-element `Array` values into in-memory columns is now faster. This happens, for example, during joins. [#117321](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117321) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* With `use_top_k_dynamic_filtering = 1` (the default), a `SELECT ... ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` could read far more data than with the setting off: the internal `__topKFilter` took over the read's `PREWHERE` slot and displaced the query's own condition, and an explicit `PREWHERE` disabled the feature outright. The threshold filter now shares `PREWHERE` with the other conditions and takes the first slot. It is also no longer installed on a read that a sorting projection already delivers in `ORDER BY` order, where it forced a full scan. [#117968](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117968) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Granules fully filtered by the dynamic threshold of an `ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` read are now recorded in the query condition cache, so repeat runs of such queries skip them at the mark-selection stage instead of re-reading and re-filtering most of the table. On the ClickBench Q24/Q26 shapes (`SELECT SearchPhrase FROM hits WHERE SearchPhrase <> '' ORDER BY EventTime LIMIT 10`), a warm run drops from about 95 million rows / 12000 granules read to about 1.3 million rows / 160 granules, and from about 15-30 ms to about 8 ms on a 96-core machine. This now also works for `ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` queries over `Parquet` files of `File` tables, with or without a `WHERE` clause: a repeated query reads only the row groups which can hold rows of its result. [#114659](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114659) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#122064](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122064) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Improve performance of `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` queries that use minmax-index top-K pruning (`use_skip_indexes_for_top_k`) on large tables with many granules. [#116512](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116512) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Queries with `FINAL` that sort in reverse order of the sorting key, such as `SELECT ... FROM t FINAL ORDER BY key DESC LIMIT n`, now read in order through `Merge` tables over `ReplacingMergeTree` tables too, and the `topKThroughJoin` optimization defers to reverse-order reads below a `LEFT JOIN` when the table supports them. This extends [#115345](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115345). [#118008](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118008) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Fixed a performance regression in `ORDER BY` merge sort: queries with a sort followed by `LIMIT` no longer do redundant per-output-block work in result-block reservation. [#108179](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/108179) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Setting `optimize_move_to_prewhere_if_final` is now enabled by default, so queries with `FINAL` benefit from the automatic `PREWHERE` optimization. Only deterministic conditions over the sorting key are moved, and query results are not affected. [#121350](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121350) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* With short-circuit evaluation, non-throwing arguments of `and` and `or` are now ordered by measured cost and selectivity, so cheap filters can avoid evaluating expensive expressions on excluded rows. Controlled by `short_circuit_function_evaluation_reorder_arguments`, enabled by default. [#122508](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122508) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Fix a performance regression in short-circuit evaluation of `and` and `or` by avoiding expansion of intermediate results that are only used to update the logical mask. [#120520](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120520) ([LB7666](https://github.com/acking-you)).
* On a table with no applicable data skipping index, a query no longer stores the same predicate under two query condition cache keys. This halves the number of `O(marks per part)` cache entries such a query allocates, and removes one cache lookup per part. Queries with `distributed_index_analysis` enabled keep both keys. [#116259](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116259) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `SELECT ... FINAL` with a selective skip-index plan is much faster on tables with many granules: the `use_skip_indexes_if_final_exact_mode` recovery pass now iterates ranges instead of individual granules. [#121799](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121799) ([Nikolai Kochetov](https://github.com/KochetovNicolai)).
* Improve primary index analysis in queries that filter both by the primary key and by other columns, for example `WHERE timestamp BETWEEN ... AND level = 'error'` where `level` is not in the primary key. [#123535](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123535) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* `IS NULL` and `IS NOT NULL` predicates can now prune `MergeTree` parts using the `NULL` row count tracked by `basic` statistics on `Nullable` columns. [#116697](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116697) ([zoomxi](https://github.com/zoomxi)).
* A `set` skip index on a `Nullable` column again skips granules whose stored set contains `NULL` but not the searched value for `IN` and `=` conditions, restoring the pruning lost in 26.8. Closes [#123705](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123705). [#123778](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123778) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Skip indexes (`text`, `bloom_filter`, `tokenbf_v1`, `ngrambf_v1`, `minmax`) and the primary key are now used when the indexed column or the searched constant is wrapped in a conversion that does not change its value, such as `toLowCardinality`, `toNullable`, or a `CAST` to `Nullable` or `LowCardinality`. For example, `m['key'] = 'value'` on `Map(String, LowCardinality(String))` and `hasToken(s, toLowCardinality('x'))` no longer scan all granules. `bloom_filter` and text indexes also work when a `Nullable` indexed column is cast to the non-`Nullable` type, such as `CAST(x, 'String')` or `json.path::String`. [#120220](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120220) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)). [#123239](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123239) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)). [#124220](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124220) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Speed up planning of queries with skip indexes on tables with many parts and pending patch parts of lightweight updates. [#124931](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124931) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Speed up queries with multiple `PREWHERE` conditions on AMD Zen 1 and Zen 2 CPUs. [#120283](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120283) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Analysis of a query over a `Merge` table no longer traverses every child table for each query-tree node when `optimize_functions_to_subcolumns` is enabled. The answer is now reused across the query tree, so analysis time grows with the query size plus the number of child tables instead of their product. Closes [#113202](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/113202). [#113367](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113367) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Queries to the `merge` table function and creation of `Merge` tables are faster when the source tables have many columns: combining the source columns no longer takes quadratic time in the number of columns. [#124115](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124115) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster analysis of queries to tables with many `ALIAS` columns, such as `system.metric_log`: the expression of an `ALIAS` column is analyzed only if the query uses that column. [#123926](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123926) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Reduce aggregate projection analysis overhead for tables with many subcolumns. [#110979](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110979) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* A condition such as `name IN ('a', 'b')` on the system tables that enumerate tables (`system.tables`, `system.columns`, `system.parts`, `system.replicas`, and others) now narrows the enumeration instead of walking every table of every database, the same way an equality condition already did. This is most visible for databases whose table list comes from a remote system, such as `PostgreSQL`, `SQLite`, and the DataLake catalogs. [#117589](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117589) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the `external_storage_push_down_limit` setting to reduce data read from external databases for safe `LIMIT` queries. Also fixed wrong results when `LIMIT` plus `OFFSET` does not fit in `UInt64`: `numbers`, `system.numbers`, `generate_series`, `primes`, `system.primes`, and the `PostgreSQL` table engine returned no rows for the common "no limit" idiom `LIMIT 18446744073709551615 OFFSET n`, and a `LIMIT` over a `JOIN` with `numbers`-like sources returned too few rows. [#115238](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115238) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#118131](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118131) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Push filters into the query fragment executed by parallel replicas so replicas can filter rows and prune data before sending results to the initiator. This also allows join runtime filters to reach the relevant reads. Controlled by `parallel_replicas_filter_pushdown`, now enabled by default; `SET parallel_replicas_filter_pushdown = 0` restores the behavior before 26.10. [#117373](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117373) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* With parallel replicas, `optimize_aggregation_in_order` no longer disables projections: aggregate and normal projections, and `_minmax_count_projection` for `count()` with a partition predicate, are used as with `optimize_aggregation_in_order = 0`. Closes [#123061](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123061). [#123098](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123098) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Parallel replicas now apply `enable_join_runtime_filters_index_analysis` to prune probe-side granules locally on each replica, reducing `JOIN` read work. [#123116](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123116) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Automatic parallel replicas no longer distributes a query when most of its reading would be repeated in full by every replica rather than split between them. The limit is set by the new setting `automatic_parallel_replicas_max_duplicated_read_ratio` (default `0.5`; `1` disables it). [#123332](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123332) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Automatic parallel replicas for multi-read queries now reuse local plan index analysis in the parallel-replicas plan, reducing extra reads when parallel replicas are enabled. [#115153](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115153) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Improve reuse of collected query statistics by automatic parallel replicas for equivalent local and distributed plans. [#116781](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116781) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Automatic parallel replicas now account correctly for the size of aggregated results on more aggregation paths, so the cost model no longer treats shipping aggregate states as free and is less likely to choose parallel replicas when a query is cheaper locally. [#119499](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119499) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Automatic parallel replicas now collect planning statistics for the table read that is actually split between replicas, including after join reordering and with lazy materialization, improving the decision to distribute a query. [#122906](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122906) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Avoid building the parallel replicas candidate plan when cached statistics already show the query should stay on a single replica. [#124983](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124983) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Added quick checks to skip automatic parallel replicas optimization for queries unsupported by parallel replicas, including keyless aggregations whose aggregate state owns memory and therefore never produces the probe statistics the optimization needs. [#120861](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120861) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)). [#125470](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125470) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Automatic parallel replicas (`automatic_parallel_replicas_mode`) can now be applied to queries with `ORDER BY ... WITH FILL`. [#123929](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123929) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Queries that search for several tokens at once with a text index, such as `hasAllTokens`, are now faster. The automatic choice of algorithm for intersecting posting lists is improved too, and is controlled by the new setting `text_index_postings_intersection_algorithm`. [#121038](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121038) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Queries that use a text index read large posting lists faster and with fewer disk operations. The improvement is most noticeable for searches for frequent tokens in big tables. [#121040](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121040) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* A query that searches a text index and also selects a column no index covers no longer reads that column for every row of every granule the index selected. On a 1M-row table with a 1055-byte payload and 64 matching rows, `SELECT ts, payload FROM t WHERE hasToken(message, 'needle')` now reads 578 KiB instead of 532 MiB. [#120517](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120517) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A query that reads a `text` index no longer asks the filesystem for each index substream's on-disk name and size once per part; both are now taken from the part's checksums, which already hold them. On an 8M-row table split into 400 parts, `SELECT count() FROM logs WHERE hasToken(message, 'needle')` drops from a median 12 ms to 9 ms, with 42% less system time. [#120875](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120875) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up queries that filter by a text index together with other `PREWHERE` conditions, such as the dynamic filter of `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` queries. [#124880](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124880) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Speed up `LIKE`, `ILIKE`, `startsWith`, and `endsWith` evaluated by a text-index dictionary scan (`use_text_index_like_evaluation_by_dictionary_scan`): the scan now seeks the sorted dictionary to the block range an anchored pattern can match, and tests a block's tokens for the pattern's mandatory literal in one pass instead of running the matcher on every token. [#120495](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120495) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Repeated broad `LIKE` and `ILIKE` queries over a text index no longer rescan the index dictionary of every part only to abandon the scan again: the decision is cached (setting `use_text_index_pattern_bypass_cache`). [#113572](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113572) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* A text index with the `splitByNonAlpha` or `array` tokenizer now answers single-needle `multiSearchAny`, `multiSearchAnyUTF8`, `multiSearchAnyCaseInsensitive`, and `multiSearchAnyCaseInsensitiveUTF8` with the same dictionary scan that `LIKE '%needle%'` uses, so a needle inside a word, such as `PointerExc` in `NullPointerException`, no longer reads the whole column. [#124520](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124520) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `multiSearchAny` over a map element can now use a `mapValues(...)` ngram or token text index instead of being rejected by index analysis. [#121787](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121787) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Optimize `SELECT count()` queries answered from a text index with a compressed posting list codec (`bitpacking`, `pfor`). [#123294](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123294) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Reduced the CPU overhead of reading and intersecting text index posting lists in lazy apply mode. [#123786](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123786) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)). [#123873](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123873) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Merges of tables with a `text` index are now faster. Pages of a source part's row ids that hold consecutive values are stored as a first value plus a count, so they are neither bit-packed nor decoded per posting row id and need no storage for packed values. [#120982](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120982) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Merging positional text indexes is faster: position lists are now merged segment by segment instead of being materialized and sorted in a temporary `roaringish` structure. [#121976](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121976) ([Elmi Ahmadov](https://github.com/ahmadov)).
* Added the `pfor` codec for text-index posting lists. It improves compression ratio and decoding speed. Enable it per index with `posting_list_codec = 'pfor'`, or for all text indexes of a table with the `text_index_posting_list_codec` setting. [#118123](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118123) ([Elmi Ahmadov](https://github.com/ahmadov)).
* Building text indexes during `INSERT` is now faster, especially for indexes with the `bitpacking` codec. [#113856](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113856) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Building a text index over a `LowCardinality(String)` or `Array(LowCardinality(String))` column is faster: each distinct value is tokenized once per batch of rows instead of at every occurrence. `MATERIALIZE INDEX` over a column with 50 distinct values takes about 33% less time. [#122409](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122409) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Improve performance of the `ngrams` tokenizer. This speeds up `tokens(s, 'ngrams', N)`, `ngrams(s, N)`, `hasAnyTokens`, `hasAllTokens`, and `hasPhrase` with the `ngrams` tokenizer, as well as inserts into tables with an `ngrambf_v1` or `text(tokenizer = ngrams(N))` index. `tokens(URL, 'ngrams', 3)` over ClickBench URLs takes 26% (aarch64) to 36% (x86\_64) less time. [#122328](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122328) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading columns and subcolumns of `MergeTree` tables no longer rebuilds the serialization of a column for every reader: the serialization the data part already holds is reused. The gain is largest for `JSON` columns with many typed paths, where the whole set of path serializations used to be rebuilt per read task and per filter step. [#120632](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120632) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* Reduce per-block overhead when reading `MergeTree` parts with small blocks: column-stream names and substream-cache keys are no longer rebuilt for every block, and reads skip patch-part handling when there are none, virtual-column name lookups, and some small-column reallocations. This makes queries with a low `max_block_size` faster. [#124816](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124816) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)). [#124817](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124817) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Reduce CPU overhead of reads from `MergeTree` tables by not formatting a tracing span name for every block. [#124820](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124820) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Reading many columns from Compact parts is up to 2% faster by avoiding per-call copies of column metadata. [#122840](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122840) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* Restore parallel prefix deserialization when reading an `Object` or `JSON` column from a Wide part: marks loading no longer queues behind one mutex. Closes [#114136](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/114136). [#114252](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114252) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Queries that read subcolumns of `JSON` columns with many typed paths are faster: resolving a subcolumn by name visits only the typed paths that can match it. For a `JSON` column with 500 typed paths, reading subcolumns from 96 parts takes about half the time. [#122029](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122029) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* Reduce serialization construction when reading `JSON` subcolumns from `MergeTree` parts by reusing safe cached subcolumn serializations. [#110957](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110957) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* Improve nested `JSON` path read performance with `map_with_buckets` by reusing thread-local serializations for `Dynamic` values. [#113344](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113344) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* Writing `JSON` columns with many paths in shared data is faster: the writer no longer rebuilds the statistics of the column for every block. For merges of parts with small granules, this saves up to 13% of the merge time. [#121987](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121987) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* Make constructing a `JSON` data type that uses `SKIP REGEXP` cheaper. The patterns are now compiled once per type instead of once for every declared path, so a column declaring 183 typed paths and 6 `SKIP REGEXP` entries performs 6 regular expression compilations where it previously performed 1098. This matters most for wide `JSON` schemas, where the patterns are often long alternations that are expensive to compile. [#122941](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122941) ([arc1999](https://github.com/arc1999)).
* Read only the matching `Map` subcolumn for safe `LIKE` predicates over `Map` values. [#119809](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119809) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Improve `SELECT` performance by avoiding lock contention when getting mutation snapshots with many concurrent background fetches. [#102382](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/102382) ([c-end](https://github.com/c-end)).
* Support `PREWHERE` (including the automatic move of `WHERE` conditions by `optimize_move_to_prewhere`) for `Memory` tables: only the columns of the conditions are read at first, and the remaining columns are read only for the blocks where some rows pass, and only for the passing rows. This is especially beneficial for tables with `SETTINGS compress = true`, because for a selective condition most columns are never decompressed. Additionally, `SELECT count() FROM table` on a `Memory` table is now served from metadata, and `system.columns` shows real per-column sizes for `Memory` tables. [#116248](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116248) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `PREWHERE` over a `Memory` table is evaluated in multiple steps, like for `MergeTree` (`enable_multiple_prewhere_read_steps`): the columns of each next condition are read only for the rows that passed the previous ones. For a table with `compress = true`, a condition on a wide column next to a selective one no longer decompresses that column for every block, which makes such queries up to several times faster. [#122604](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122604) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Queries with `ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` over `Memory` tables use TopN dynamic filtering: rows that cannot get into the result are dropped while reading, before the other columns are read or decompressed with `compress = true`. [#123787](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123787) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Queries whose only aggregate functions are `min` and `max` of columns of `Parquet` files read with the `file` table function or the `File` engine, without `GROUP BY` or filters (for example ClickBench Q7), now take the values from the column chunk statistics instead of reading the data. Controlled by the new setting `optimize_min_max_from_files`. [#124201](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124201) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` over a single `Parquet` file reads first the row groups whose min/max statistics are best for the result, so the top-K threshold skips the other row groups: `SELECT URL FROM file('hits.parquet') ORDER BY UserID DESC LIMIT 10` skips 207 of its 226 row groups at 4 threads. New profile event `ParquetTopKSkippedRowGroups`. [#123639](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123639) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Row group pruning by dictionaries in the `Parquet` reader is faster for an equality or a short `IN` list on a column with large dictionaries, such as `WHERE UserID = ...` over ClickBench `hits.parquet`: the hashes of the dictionary values are no longer sorted unless a column chunk is checked against many values. [#123579](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123579) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `Parquet` readers now check a column chunk's bloom filter before reading its dictionary page. A row group that the bloom filter rules out no longer reads the much larger dictionary page to reach the same conclusion, so `input_format_parquet_dictionary_filter_push_down` costs nothing on files that carry both, while pruning as much as before. [#120907](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120907) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `Parquet` footer-size reads are now tuned by storage type: remote and object-storage reads use more optimistic footer-size estimates to fetch the footer in one read instead of two, reducing footer-read latency, while local-file reads again use a 64 KiB tail so short queries over many local `Parquet` files read less data. [#115686](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115686) ([UnamedRus](https://github.com/UnamedRus)). [#124883](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124883) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Avoid a redundant object-storage GET request when reading random-access formats from object storage. The generic from-start read-ahead is no longer issued for files larger than a single read buffer, where it could not reach the footer. Also, `input_format_allow_seeks = 0` is now honored by the `Parquet` reader, which previously sought regardless of the setting: with seeks disabled the whole object is read sequentially instead, which can raise memory usage for queries that set it. [#115681](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115681) ([UnamedRus](https://github.com/UnamedRus)).
* Speed up writing `Parquet` bloom filters: instead of deduplicating the value hashes in a hash set to determine the filter size, the writer now builds a filter sized for the number of values and folds it down to the size implied by `output_format_parquet_bloom_filter_bits_per_value`. This removes the worst-case slowdown on data that is adversarial for the hash set, and lowers the worst-case memory usage. [#119230](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119230) ([Armin Braun](https://github.com/original-brownbear)).
* Parse some GeoParquet metadata lazily. [#122693](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122693) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Snappy decompression on AArch64 now uses NEON for short-offset copies, as upstream snappy does by default, which makes reading Snappy-compressed `Parquet` with repetitive string columns up to \~9% faster on ARM servers. The ARMv8.0 compatibility build (`NO_ARMV81_OR_HIGHER`) is unchanged. [#124608](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124608) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading `CSV` with `input_format_csv_trim_whitespaces = 0` no longer prevents the parser from scaling across CPU cores. Each field used to copy a `shared_ptr` shared by all parsing threads, turning its reference counter into a contention point. On a 96-core machine, reading 200 million rows became 4.6 times faster and used 23 times less CPU. [#117251](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117251) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `Iceberg` partition pruning now uses the partition value of a `day`, `month`, `year`, or `hour` transform for a filter that wraps the partition source column in a monotonic function, such as `WHERE toDate(ts) = '2024-01-20'`, including for partition values from before 1970. Closes [#103433](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/103433). [#118606](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118606) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)). [#119302](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119302) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Faster planning of queries to `Iceberg` tables with many manifest files: when a manifest file header has the `schema-id` key and that schema is already known from `metadata.json`, the schema copy stored in the manifest header is no longer parsed and compared with it. ClickHouse now also writes the `schema-id` key into the headers of manifest files it creates. [#122574](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122574) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* An `INSERT` into an `Iceberg` table that loses the commit race now reuses its manifests on retry and rewrites only the manifest list and the metadata file, instead of regenerating all manifests. [#122096](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122096) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)).
* Restore the summary-based trivial `count()` optimization for `Iceberg` tables when the snapshot summary contains a safe row count. The optimization is skipped when equality deletes are present, because their recorded count is not the number of deleted rows. [#123267](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123267) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* `Iceberg` min/max hyperrectangles are now built only for columns the filter uses. [#122681](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122681) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Restore min/max pruning of `Iceberg` data files for a `Decimal(38, 38)` column. A manifest bound of magnitude above roughly 0.7 could not be widened inside `Int128` and was dropped, leaving the column without a min/max condition; it is now saturated at the largest magnitude the precision allows, which is still outside every value the file can hold. [#119712](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119712) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Queries to `system.iceberg_history` with filters on `table` now avoid listing unrelated data lake catalog namespaces. [#113867](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113867) ([Den Kalantaevskii](https://github.com/Diskein)).
* Improve HTTP upload and download efficiency for `S3`, `Azure`, the `url` table function and engine, dictionaries, and data lake catalogs. Uploads use 1 MiB chunks instead of 8 KiB chunks, reducing the number of system calls for a 15 MB body from 1817 to 15 and avoiding redundant copies. Reads from `Azure` also avoid an intermediate 8 KiB buffer. [#116222](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116222) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Reading from object storage with `remote_filesystem_read_method = 'read'` now keeps full-size network reads. Before, a short read at the end of one range capped every later read of the same file, which could make a scan hundreds of times slower, for example a compact part on an encrypted `S3` disk without the filesystem cache. [#123777](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123777) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reads that are served from the OS page cache no longer consume the budget of the local read bandwidth throttler (`max_local_read_bandwidth_for_server` and `max_local_read_bandwidth`), so a query over cached data is not slowed down to the configured disk bandwidth. This works with the default `local_filesystem_read_method = 'pread_threadpool'`, with `pread`, and for the files of the filesystem cache; the `read` method cannot detect cached reads and is accounted as before. [#118495](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118495) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Populating the filesystem cache on a cache miss is faster: the writer now reserves space ahead instead of taking the cache lock on every write. [#123255](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123255) ([Kseniia Sumarokova](https://github.com/kssenii)).
* Loading the metadata of a large `s3_plain_rewritable` disk is several times faster. Previously it made one `ListObjects` request per directory and enumerated the `__meta` directory by a single paginated listing, so a disk with tens of thousands of directories needed tens of thousands of sequential-ish requests. The listings are now split by key prefix and run in parallel, and the files of all directories are collected by a few listings of the whole disk. For a disk with 36 thousand directories holding half a million objects, the load time dropped from 116 to 37 seconds. [#121412](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121412) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Metadata transactions of unrelated tables on a `plain_rewritable` disk no longer wait for each other: the disk-wide lock used to be held across all object storage requests of a commit, so one long `MOVE PARTITION`, `DROP TABLE`, or part removal blocked every merge, insert, and `CREATE TABLE` on the same disk. The lock is now taken per affected path, and the in-memory metadata is updated by replaying the recorded changes. Also fixes a permanent leak of objects when the server was killed during `DROP TABLE` (or another removal) on a `plain_rewritable` disk: the leftovers of such a removal are now marked and deleted when the disk metadata is loaded on the next start. [#118490](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118490) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Removing data parts from a disk with `metadata_type = plain_rewritable` (cleanup of outdated parts, `TRUNCATE`, `DROP PARTITION`, `DROP TABLE`) no longer copies every file of the part to a temporary object before deleting it, and no longer holds the disk metadata lock for those copies. The part directory is removed with one batched delete request. Closes [#123275](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123275). [#123303](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123303) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reduce metadata update cost and lock contention for `s3_plain_rewritable` disks with many parts by sharing unchanged directory entries between snapshots. [#119625](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119625) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* Avoid object reads for unchanged directories when a plain-rewritable disk refreshes its metadata. [#119760](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119760) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* `OPTIMIZE TABLE ... FINAL` on a non-replicated `MergeTree` table now assigns and runs the merges for all partitions at once instead of processing them one partition at a time. [#109004](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/109004) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `TTL DELETE` merges can now use the vertical merge algorithm on `ReplacingMergeTree`, `CollapsingMergeTree`, and `VersionedCollapsingMergeTree`, where `vertical_merge_optimize_ttl_delete` previously applied only to plain `MergeTree`. [#119886](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119886) ([Murphy](https://github.com/murphy-4o)).
* Allow vertical merges to produce `Wide` parts with `Packed` storage instead of forcing the horizontal merge algorithm. [#125760](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125760) ([alesapin](https://github.com/alesapin)).
* Improved merge performance for `SummingMergeTree` tables by skipping unnecessary aggregate-column materialization. [#120852](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120852) ([Eduard Karacharov](https://github.com/korowa)).
* Many queued lightweight deletes with an `IN` condition that reach the same part in one mutation are now applied together, instead of evaluating each `IN` predicate over the whole part separately. Controlled by the new `MergeTree` setting `enable_row_mask_update_coalescing`. [#122718](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122718) ([Sergey Kuznetsov](https://github.com/skuznetsov-clickhouse)).
* Optimize the insert backpressure path in `MergeTree` by short-circuiting the parts-count check. [#114754](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114754) ([johnjing](https://github.com/JingYanchao)).
* Reduced merge-selection work when all storage volumes allow merges. [#119782](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119782) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* Reduce merge-selection CPU overhead for tables with column TTLs by avoiding copies of TTL descriptions. [#119644](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119644) ([Rory Shanks](https://github.com/rorylshanks)).
* Speed up decompression of codec chains. The first codec now reads the compressed block in place and the last codec writes the result in place, unless that codec requires a padded buffer (`LZ4`, `ZXC`). [#120043](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120043) ([Raufs Dunamalijevs](https://github.com/rienath)).
* Faster decompression of `T64`-encoded columns. [#120414](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120414) ([Raufs Dunamalijevs](https://github.com/rienath)). [#122442](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122442) ([Raufs Dunamalijevs](https://github.com/rienath)).
* A query with `arrayJoin` and a small `LIMIT` again shrinks the read block size instead of reading whole blocks on every thread for a few output rows, fixing a regression from 26.6. Closes [#119193](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/119193). [#120214](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120214) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Chained `ARRAY JOIN`s no longer materialize the whole block of a lazily replicated array column before expanding it, which cuts their peak memory by several times. [#121397](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121397) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Avoid copying whole columns when slicing them, including in `ARRAY JOIN`. [#118788](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118788) ([Nikita Mikhaylov](https://github.com/nikitamikhaylov)).
* Improve `ARRAY JOIN` performance for queries with wide columns that are not expanded, by avoiding unnecessary copies and preserving lazy replication through filtering and sorting. [#124727](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124727) ([Yarik Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Reduced the peak memory usage of `RESTORE` and of incremental `BACKUP`: the metadata file of a backup is now parsed while it is read, instead of being read into memory whole first. For backups of millions of files this saves gigabytes. [#125712](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125712) ([Julia Kartseva](https://github.com/jkartseva)).
* Faster startup of `clickhouse-local` and other tools. Time zone lookup tables are built 2.4 to 5.6 times faster and use half the memory (2.3 MiB instead of 4.6 MiB per loaded time zone). The `clickhouse` binary also starts faster when it is not in the page cache, with a 1.40x speedup measured for `clickhouse local`. [#125665](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125665) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)). [#125672](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125672) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* A full scan of a complex-key `hashed`, `sparse_hashed`, `hashed_array`, or `range_hashed` dictionary (`SELECT ... FROM <dictionary>` or the `dictionary` table function) is faster: each reading stream now deserializes only the keys of its own block instead of one thread deserializing all keys before reading starts. A scan with `LIMIT` deserializes only the blocks it reads. A full scan also no longer keeps all deserialized keys in memory at once. [#123883](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123883) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Added the `SIEVE` cache eviction policy for in-memory caches such as the mark cache and the uncompressed cache. Enable it with the corresponding `*_cache_policy` server settings, for example `mark_cache_policy` or `uncompressed_cache_policy`. It is not available for the filesystem cache. [#123435](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123435) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The symbolized stack trace of a failed query is now built only when the error message will be logged or the trace will be stored in `system.query_log`. Previously each failing `clickhouse-local` invocation built the symbol index of the whole binary to produce a string that was then discarded. [#120660](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120660) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reduces the CPU overhead of the workload scheduler thread by updating the `system.scheduler` introspection metrics more cheaply. A saturated scheduler can now serve about 15% more resource requests per second. [#121958](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121958) ([Sergei Trifonov](https://github.com/serxa)).
* PromQL now copies label matchers across binary operators for the labels the operator matches series by, so `b / on(job) a{job="x"}` reads only the matching series of `b`. As in Prometheus, duplicate left series of a one-to-one match in a group the right side does not have no longer fail the query. The new setting `promql_push_down_label_matchers` turns this off. [#122972](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122972) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)). [#125267](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125267) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Accelerate exact PromQL label matching on `TimeSeries` tables with a text index on the `tags` map. [#120083](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120083) ([Nikita Mikhaylov](https://github.com/nikitamikhaylov)).
* Use more compact codecs for generated `timestamp` and `value` columns in the samples and recent samples tables of the `TimeSeries` engine: `Delta`, `T64`, and `ZSTD(3)` for `timestamp`, and `ALP` plus `ZSTD(3)` for `value`. [#120454](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120454) ([Nikita Mikhaylov](https://github.com/nikitamikhaylov)).
* Reduce memory usage of the `timeSeries*ToGrid` aggregate functions. [#124773](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124773) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* Filtering and sorting are now faster: the helpers that build filter masks, count filter bytes, and check for an identity permutation use AVX2 or AVX-512 on x86 instead of SSE2, and are vectorized on ARM where they were not. [#121403](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121403) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Faster filtering of numeric columns on ARM (up to 1.28x) and on AMD Zen 4 (up to 2.8x), speeding up queries with `WHERE` and multi-step `PREWHERE`. [#122893](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122893) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Filters on numeric columns that keep few rows are faster. [#124024](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124024) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Filtering numeric columns no longer over-allocates the result buffer (by up to 2x) when the size of the result is known in advance. Closes [#123546](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123546). [#123576](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123576) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Vectorized comparisons of `UUID`, `IPv4`, and `IPv6` columns with columns or constants of the same type, which makes such comparisons several times faster. Comparisons of `FixedString(16)` values are now branchless too, making them faster when it is unpredictable whether the values are equal. [#120193](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120193) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Comparisons `<`, `>`, `<=`, and `>=` of `IPv6` and `FixedString(16)` values are 1.4 to 1.8 times faster on CPUs with AVX-512. [#125151](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125151) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `LIKE`, `ILIKE`, `match`, and their variants are faster when the pattern is not a constant, up to 5 times with 8 threads. [#122764](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122764) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)). [#122843](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122843) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* Faster `multiMatchAny` and related functions with non-constant needles when `reject_expensive_hyperscan_regexps` is disabled. [#122842](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122842) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* `CAST` of strings to `Nullable(Bool)` (also `accurateCastOrNull(s, 'Bool')` and `toBool` of a `Nullable(String)`) is up to 7 times faster with 8 threads, and `arrayIntersect`, `arrayUnion`, `arraySymmetricDifference`, and `arrayJaccardIndex` over non-numeric elements such as `DateTime64`, `UUID`, or `Tuple` are up to 18 times faster with 8 threads: they no longer allocate memory and update profile events for every row. `multiMatchAny` and the other multi-regexp functions with non-constant needles now add their cache hit and miss counts once per block. [#122920](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122920) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up extracting a value from a `Map` with `LowCardinality` keys. The key is now resolved to its dictionary position once instead of being compared against every entry of a row, so the cost of `m[key]` no longer grows with the position of the key in the row. Also fix extracting a value from a `Map` whose key is `NaN`, or holds a `NULL` inside a composite key: such a key used to compare equal to every other key. [#120453](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120453) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* `L2DistanceTransposed`, `cosineDistanceTransposed`, `dotProductTransposed`, and their `...Quantized` variants with precision `1` are now computed directly from the packed sign bit plane with per-byte lookup tables, 2 to 3 times faster than before. The new setting `qbit_one_bit_symmetric_distance` additionally reduces the reference vector to its signs and derives the result from the Hamming distance between the two sign vectors, computed with XOR and popcount, which is up to 10 times faster. [#120594](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120594) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Speed up `randomHadamardTransform` and vector quantization for vector dimensions of the form 2^k × 12 or 2^k × 20, such as 768 or 1536, on x86 with AVX2: 1.9x to 2.5x higher throughput and 50% to 60% lower median latency. [#120874](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120874) ([Jingxin (Philip) Li](https://github.com/philip-jingxin)).
* `hasAll` and `hasAny` on integer arrays use SIMD on both x86 and ARM, including `Nullable` arrays and `hasAny`, which previously used a scalar loop. `hasAny` is up to 15x faster, and `hasAll` becomes several times faster on ARM. [#123420](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123420) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `hasAny` over `Array(String)` looks each element up in a hash set, instead of comparing it with every string, when its second argument is a constant array of four or more strings of up to 256 bytes and a block has enough rows to pay for building the set. With 32 strings this uses 87% less CPU. Closes [#122333](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122333). [#122507](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122507) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Vectorize `least` and `greatest` for `Nullable` numeric arguments and for three or more numeric arguments, and `clamp` for numeric arguments. These cases were previously evaluated row-by-row and are now 3-7 times faster. [#109783](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/109783) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Speed up `round`, `roundBankers`, `floor`, `ceil`, and `trunc` with a scale argument on `Float32` and `Float64` columns by using AVX2 on x86 instead of SSE4.1, and speed up rounding of integers narrower than 64 bits on ARM by vectorizing it. [#122032](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122032) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* `modulo` and `moduloOrNull` of two unsigned integers of different widths are faster again, as before 26.9, when the wider one is `UInt128` (about 3 to 4 times, for example `UInt128 % UInt16`) or the dividend is `UInt256` (1.6 to 2 times). [#123170](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123170) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `exp10` is up to 2x faster by splitting its argument with `trunc` instead of the `modf` library call, with bitwise-identical results. [#122263](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122263) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Speed up `entropy` and multi-argument `uniqExact` on short strings by optimizing `SipHash` for short inputs. Hash values are unchanged. [#123658](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123658) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `sumMap`, `minMap`, `maxMap`, `sumMapWithOverflow`, and `sumMapFiltered` over arrays are up to 2.3 times faster for integer, `Enum`, `Date`, `Date32`, and `DateTime` keys. [#124340](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124340) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `h3PolygonToCells` and `h3PolygonToCellsWithContainment` are several times faster on polygons much smaller than a cell (9x on the query from the issue). This brings `h3PolygonToCells` back to the speed it had on such polygons before 26.8.12 and 26.9. Closes [#123877](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123877). [#123911](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123911) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `isValidUTF8` validates a `String` or `FixedString` column with one `simdutf` call over the whole column instead of one call per row, which makes it up to 2.8 times faster on short strings. [#123835](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123835) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up `lowerUTF8` and `upperUTF8` on text whose non-ASCII characters are mostly two-byte UTF-8 (`U+0080` to `U+07FF`, e.g. Latin-1, Greek, Cyrillic): the column is processed in one pass, these characters are mapped from a table built from ICU, and ICU is called only for the rest of a row that contains other characters. [#123867](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123867) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `lower`, `upper`, and the ASCII path of `lowerUTF8` and `upperUTF8` are now faster by using AVX2 or AVX-512 depending on the CPU. [#121374](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121374) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Speed up `lengthUTF8` and other `UTF-8` functions on long strings by counting code points 64 bytes at a time, on both x86 and ARM. [#122049](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122049) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Fixed ARM performance regressions in `indexOf` with a constant needle and in text output formats over non-ASCII strings. Closes [#123338](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123338). Closes [#123346](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/123346). [#123377](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123377) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* UTF-8 string functions such as `substringUTF8` are faster on long ASCII strings on CPUs with AVX-512. [#120088](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120088) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Improved performance of functions `extractKeyValuePairs` and `extractKeyValuePairsWithEscaping`. [#119361](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119361) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Function `mortonEncode` now resolves the type of each argument and the ratios of a constant range mask once per block instead of once per row, which makes it up to 2x faster. [#123354](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123354) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up `mortonDecode` on AArch64 by decoding with compile-time shift-and-mask sequences instead of a lookup table. The output is unchanged on every architecture. [#116205](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116205) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `encrypt` and `aes_encrypt_mysql` are faster on values shorter than 16 bytes in the `aes-*-ecb` and `aes-*-cbc` modes (up to 2x for `encrypt` with `aes-128-ecb`). [#124103](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124103) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up `hex` and `unhex` with optimized `AVX` and `AVX2` implementations when they are available. [#123511](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123511) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Faster `toMillisecond`, `toMicrosecond`, and `toNanosecond` on `DateTime64`. [#122852](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122852) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* Speed up `reverse` for `String` and `FixedString` values. [#119462](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119462) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* `arrayDistinct` is faster: it now does a single hash-table lookup per element instead of two, and `Decimal` arrays use a specialized fast path. [#113966](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113966) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)). [#121646](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121646) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Specialize `has` and `indexOf` searches in non-nullable numeric arrays when the needle is constant. [#119090](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119090) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* `arrayFilter` is faster when the lambda keeps all or none of the elements of a block. [#119816](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119816) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Improve performance of `bitmapAndCardinality`, `bitmapOrCardinality`, `bitmapXorCardinality`, `bitmapAndnotCardinality`, and `bitmapHasAny` for `UInt64` and `Int64` bitmaps, which previously had to materialize the intersection to compute the result. On AArch64, `bitmapAndCardinality`, `bitmapOrCardinality`, `bitmapXorCardinality`, and `bitmapAndnotCardinality` no longer hit a rare code-layout-dependent slow path. [#120615](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120615) ([Ashish kumar choubey](https://github.com/aschoube)). [#123675](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123675) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Restored the existing `Decimal` fast paths in `arrayCompact`. [#121245](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121245) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* `uniqUpTo` now stops hashing after it exceeds its threshold. [#121247](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121247) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Restored the `FixedString` fast path for `arrayEnumerateUniq` and `arrayEnumerateDense`. [#121338](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121338) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* `arrayReverse` is now faster for `Decimal` and wide integer arrays. [#121342](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121342) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)). [#121644](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121644) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Avoid copying `String` columns for `substring(s, 1)`. [#119820](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119820) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Skip KMP preprocessing for impossible `hasSubstr` searches. [#119824](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119824) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Speed up `arrayMin` and `arrayMax` for `Decimal` and `DateTime64` arrays. [#123507](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123507) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Speed up `arrayUniq` for `Decimal32`, `Decimal64`, and `Decimal128` arrays. [#122121](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122121) ([Minh Vu](https://github.com/fallintoplace)).
* Allow a single condition to constrain multiple key columns during index analysis. For example, if the primary key is `(toYYYYMMDD(dt), toDate(dt), dt)` and the query has `WHERE dt >= toDateTime('2026-01-10 00:00:00')`, all three key columns are now used for pruning, instead of only one of them as before. This behavior is controlled by the setting `analyze_index_with_multiple_key_columns_per_condition` (enabled by default). [#96225](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/96225) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Left-side columns absent from a hash join's output (for example the join key in `SELECT count() ... LEFT JOIN ...`) are now pruned when only a spill threshold is set, as with the default `max_bytes_ratio_before_external_join`, instead of being carried through the pipeline. [#108191](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/108191) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Joins on null-safe comparison (`<=>`) of nullable keys use fixed-width hash keys instead of a serialized tuple. [#120596](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120596) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Fixed a quadratic slowdown of `JOIN` with `join_algorithm = 'hash'` when `ON` has a non-equality condition or several `OR` conditions. [#121209](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121209) ([nt](https://github.com/namtran1812)).
* Faster parsing of floating-point numbers from text formats. Update `fast_float` to v8.3.2. [#121383](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121383) ([Actuele AI](https://github.com/actueleai)).
* Query plan optimizations can now use the sort order reported by any table engine, not only `MergeTree`, avoiding unnecessary sorting. [#121858](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121858) ([Ivan Zhukov](https://github.com/zhkvia)).
* Slightly reduce the per-block overhead of aggregation by not copying the columns of the block when preparing the aggregate function instructions. [#124819](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124819) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `GROUP BY` by several keys, which requires the serialization of keys, serializes the keys of a block in chunks that fit in the CPU cache instead of all at once. This makes such queries faster when many threads are used, for example, ClickBench Q18 is 37% faster on a 96-core machine. [#124955](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124955) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the setting `cluster_function_wait_for_replica_ms` (default `0`, disabled), which lets cluster table functions wait for the replica assigned a file to connect before giving that file to another replica. This improves file distribution when replicas start at different times. Work stealing now takes files from the active replica with the most remaining bytes without scanning all unprocessed files. [#125611](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125611) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* The `SLRU` filesystem cache policy now moves a partially downloaded file segment to the protected queue on a cache hit, as it does for a fully downloaded one. Before, a scan could evict the data of narrow reads from the probationary queue. [#125657](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125657) ([Sema Checherinda](https://github.com/CheSema)).

#### Improvement

* Lightweight updates (the `UPDATE` statement and patch parts) are now generally available instead of Beta. The `enable_lightweight_update` setting is now in the production feature tier. [#125719](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125719) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Cluster discovery is no longer experimental: clusters with a `discovery` section in `remote_servers` work without enabling `allow_experimental_cluster_discovery`. The setting is now obsolete. [#123917](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123917) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The Kusto (KQL) and Trino query dialects were promoted from experimental to beta. They are still enabled with the `allow_experimental_kusto_dialect` and `enable_trino_dialect` settings. [#124750](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124750) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Tables and databases can now be addressed through URL paths in the HTTP interface by default: `curl http://localhost:8123/my_db/my_table.csv` now works without extra configuration. The settings `http_allow_path_requests`, `http_allow_database_as_path`, `http_allow_table_as_file`, and `http_allow_filters_as_path` are enabled by default; `http_allow_filters_as_unrecognized_url_parameters` stays disabled. [#121143](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121143) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
